- `pytest tests/test_dashboard_ui.py` — 60 passed
- `pytest tests/` — **770 passed** (60 dashboard, 34 publisher worker, 93 social, 89 wp publisher, 69 wp media/vk, 66 media, 48 fixer, 112 content, 33 content worker, 83 ingestion, 83 dedup), no external services
- `ruff check app/api/` — all checks passed; `ruff check .` — all checks passed
- `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` — success, no issues found in 55 source files

### Notes — Package 5, Micro-step 5.1

- The dashboard has **one operator and no `users` table**. A login form backed by one environment-provided credential has a far smaller attack surface than a user-management CRUD nobody needs, and the `users` table in the schema spec stays unused until there is a second operator
- The worker-health panel reports **configuration, not liveness**, and says so on the page. This process cannot see whether a separate ARQ container is alive without a heartbeat key, and a panel that implied otherwise would be worse than one that admits its limits
- Overview counters are aggregated with `GROUP BY`, never in Python: counting rows client-side would read the whole of `raw_news` — the highest-volume table in the schema — on every page load
- The articles editor is **read-only in this micro-step**, and the template says so. A save endpoint has to decide what editing a *published* article means (re-publish to WordPress? re-run distribution? leave the live post alone?), and rendering an input that silently discards edits would be worse than rendering a view
- The settings page reads keys from the environment and displays them masked. Writing keys through the UI needs a database-backed settings store, which is where the Fernet encryption will actually be applied — the `SecretBox` is implemented and tested for that
- Tests drive the app through `httpx.ASGITransport` with the lifespan **not** run: the real lifespan health-checks PostgreSQL and Redis, which is correct in production and impossible in a suite that runs with zero external services
- The templates and the SSE client have not been loaded in a real browser. The tests assert the rendered HTML and the event framing, so what is unverified is layout, Tailwind's CDN behaviour and `EventSource` reconnection against a live proxy

### Added — Package 4, Micro-step 4.3: publisher orchestrator & infinite scroll

Work performed 2026-08-23, ~17:40 to ~19:10 (UTC+03:00). **Completes Package 4.**

- `app/workers/publisher_worker.py` — the last worker in the pipeline, and its own ARQ process. Claims articles at `MEDIA_READY` under a global run lock plus a per-article lock, publishes to WordPress **first and alone**, then fans the canonical URL out to all four social adapters with `asyncio.gather(..., return_exceptions=True)`, records each verdict into `articles.platform_statuses`, and transitions the article to `DISTRIBUTED` or `WP_PUBLISHED`. A transient WordPress failure is retried with exponential backoff inside the run; an article that fails `MAX_ARTICLE_RETRIES` times is parked at `FAILED`
- `app/templates/wordpress/infinite_scroll.js` — dependency-free theme snippet. Two `IntersectionObserver` instances: one loads the next article ahead of the viewport via `rootMargin`, the other detects which article is on screen and drives `history.pushState` plus the virtual pageview. Hits go to Yandex Metrika (`window.ym`) and GA4 (`window.gtag`), each guarded so an ad blocker cannot break scrolling. `destroy()` disconnects both observers, aborts any in-flight fetch through `AbortController` and clears the seen-URL set; it is wired to `pagehide` and exported for themes that manage the lifecycle themselves
- `app/services/wp_media_service.py` — new `get_media_url(media_id)`, resolving a WordPress attachment id to its public URL

### Fixed — Package 4, Micro-step 4.3

- **Social posts would have published without a cover image.** `articles` stores only `wp_image_id`, but Telegram's `sendPhoto`, OK's photo block and Dzen's cover all need a fetchable URL. My first version of the fan-out passed a hardcoded empty `cover_url`, so every teaser on every platform would have gone out as text — roughly halving engagement — while all four adapters reported success. Found by reading the `Article` model rather than from a failing test, since nothing in the adapters could detect it. `get_media_url()` was added to the media service and the worker now resolves the cover before the fan-out, warning when the lookup fails
- Removed two unused formatter imports (`CarouselImage`, `RelatedArticle`) that my docstring claimed were populated at publication time. The docstring now states plainly that both blocks are left empty and why: the carousel needs per-image `source_url` values and the related block needs a live-article query, neither of which this worker has. Rendering empty markup on every page would be worse than omitting the blocks

### Security — Package 4, Micro-step 4.3

- **WordPress is published before any social post, unconditionally.** Every teaser links back to the article, so a Telegram post sent first would point at a 404 that cannot be recalled. A WordPress failure therefore aborts the whole distribution and leaves the article at `MEDIA_READY` for the next tick
- **Idempotency is checked at two levels.** An article not at `MEDIA_READY` is skipped outright, and a platform whose `platform_statuses` entry already reads `published` is not re-sent. Without the second check, one Telegram failure would cause duplicate VK, OK and Dzen posts on the next tick
- `return_exceptions=True` is a second containment layer beneath `safe_publish`. If a bug escapes an adapter's own error handling the gather still returns, the escaped exception is recorded as that platform's failure, and the other three keep their outcomes
- A WordPress auth failure is never retried: a rejected Application Password is rejected identically every time, and retrying only delays the run and burns the article's retry budget
- The `DOMParser` in the snippet parses fetched HTML into an **inert** document, so no script executes and no resource loads; only the one article element is adopted with `importNode`. Assigning to `innerHTML` instead would run whatever the response contained

- `pytest tests/` — **710 passed** (34 publisher worker, 93 social, 89 wp publisher, 69 wp media/vk, 66 media, 48 fixer, 112 content, 33 content worker, 83 ingestion, 83 dedup), no external services
- `ruff check .` — all checks passed

### Notes — Package 4, Micro-step 4.3

- **The task asked for `PUBLISHED` / `PARTIALLY_PUBLISHED`; neither exists in `ArticleStatus`.** Per docs/01 the states are `wp_published` (on the site) and `distributed` (social channels have run). "Partially published" is therefore expressed as `WP_PUBLISHED` *plus* the per-platform detail in `platform_statuses`, which is where an operator looks anyway. Adding synonyms would put two names for one state into the enum, the migration and every consumer
- A **disabled** platform does not block `DISTRIBUTED`. An operator who left a token unset chose not to publish there, so counting it as a failure would permanently pin every article at `WP_PUBLISHED` on a deliberately partial deployment
- `platform_statuses` is a `NestedMutableDict`, so the worker's in-place assignment dirties the row and actually flushes. The tests use a real tracked dict rather than a plain one for exactly this reason — a plain dict would accept the assignment even if the tracking were broken, and the bug would only appear in production as a silently unsaved status
- A post that WordPress creates without returning a permalink moves the article to `WP_PUBLISHED` rather than retrying it: the post *is* live, and a retry would create a duplicate. Distribution is skipped because there is no URL to distribute
- The snippet fires analytics on the **URL change**, not on the fetch. A hit sent when loading starts would count articles the reader never reached, which would make the session-depth number in Metrika meaningless
- The article and error caps in the snippet (`maxArticles`, `maxErrors`) exist because an unbounded feed exhausts memory on a long session and an unbounded retry loop hammers the origin from every open tab
- **Not verified against live services.** Every collaborator is patched at the worker's module boundary, so these 34 tests cover orchestration policy, not the platform clients — those have their own suites, themselves against mocked transports. No article has been published to a real WordPress, Telegram, VK, OK or Dzen account, and the snippet has not been loaded in a browser against a real theme: `node --check` confirms syntax only

### Added — Package 4, Micro-step 4.2: pluggable social media adapters

Work performed 2026-08-23, ~16:05 to ~17:35 (UTC+03:00).

- `app/services/social_base.py` — the shared contract. `BaseSocialAdapter` is an ABC with `publish()` and `safe_publish()`; `PublishResult` carries `platform` / `external_id` / `url` / `status` / `error` plus a `retryable` flag, and `to_platform_status()` maps it straight onto `articles.platform_statuses`. `build_teaser()` and `build_hashtags()` are shared so four adapters do not each reinvent trimming and tag normalisation
- `app/services/social_telegram.py` — Bot API adapter. `sendPhoto` with a caption when a cover exists, `sendMessage` otherwise, plus an inline `InlineKeyboardMarkup` CTA button. `caption_length()` measures in **UTF-16 code units** because that is how Telegram counts; `build_caption()` sizes the teaser from the remaining budget so the assembled caption is within 1024 by construction. A 429 is honoured using Telegram's own `parameters.retry_after`
- `app/services/social_vk.py` — `wall.post` adapter. `from_group=1` with a negative `owner_id` so the post is attributed to the community; `build_attachments()` serialises `video{owner}_{id}_{key}` / `photo{owner}_{id}` and appends the article URL so VK renders a preview card
- `app/services/social_ok.py` — `mediatopic.post` adapter. `calculate_signature()` implements the documented algorithm exactly (token excluded, keys sorted lexicographically, `key=value` joined with no separator, `MD5(joined + secret)` lowercased) and `derive_session_secret()` handles the `MD5(access_token + application_secret_key)` case. `build_media_topic()` emits the typed `media` array
- `app/services/social_dzen.py` — Dzen adapter. `build_dzen_html()` reduces the body to Dzen's accepted tag subset, `DzenAuthState` caches authentication with an expiry and a 60-second safety margin, and the publication lifecycle is an explicit draft-then-publish pair
- `app/services/vk_video_service.py` — `normalise_group_id` and `raise_for_vk_error` promoted from private to public and documented as shared, so the wall adapter and the video service cannot drift on the VK error table or on the group-id sign convention
- `tests/test_social_adapters.py` — 93 tests against `respx`

### Fixed — Package 4, Micro-step 4.2

- One of my own test defects: `test_the_dzen_auth_state_is_reused_across_publishes` read `adapter.auth_state` *after* the `finally: await adapter.close()` had already cleared it, so it asserted `None is <state>`. Both reads now happen inside the block, and the test additionally asserts that `close()` does clear the cache

### Security — Package 4, Micro-step 4.2

- **A failure is a value, not an exception.** `safe_publish()` converts every typed adapter error — and any unexpected exception — into a `PublishResult`. Four platforms publish independently, so a bug in the Dzen adapter must not stop the Telegram post; a test raises `ZeroDivisionError` inside an adapter to prove the run survives it
- **Dzen will not publish without a canonical URL.** Dzen hosts a full copy of the article, so without `canonicalUrl` its copy competes with the original in search and can outrank it — the site would donate its own traffic to the aggregator. The adapter refuses rather than publishing an uncanonical copy
- Every platform token is sent in the request body or a header, never in a query string, and no token appears in a log line or exception message. Four tests assert this, one per adapter. Telegram's is the sharpest case: the token sits in the URL *path*, so a leaked error string would leak the credential
- `MD5` in the OK adapter is the signature algorithm OK's API mandates for request authentication against their service. It is not used for storing or verifying any secret of ours, and both call sites carry a `noqa: S324` with that reasoning
- Unconfigured platforms report `DISABLED`, not `FAILED`. A missing token is a deployment choice, and reporting it as a failure would make a deliberately partial deployment look permanently broken on the dashboard — and would trigger pointless retries
- The Dzen body reduction removes `script`/`style`/`iframe`/`object`/`embed`/`form` **with their subtrees** plus every `on*` handler and inline `style`, and keeps only `href`/`src`/`alt` on surviving tags

### Notes — Package 4, Micro-step 4.2

- The OK signature is tested against a **hand-computed** MD5 rather than against `calculate_signature` itself. A signature test that calls the function it verifies proves only determinism; the independent computation is what proves the algorithm. A separate test asserts that including `access_token` in the signed set changes nothing, because leaving it in is the single most common cause of OK's opaque "invalid signature" error
- Telegram's caption cap is asserted in UTF-16 units with an emoji fixture. A `len()`-based test passes while production rejects the message, because Telegram counts the way JavaScript does and an emoji costs two
- VK attachment ids keep the owner's sign. `video-222333_777` addresses community media; dropping the minus addresses a *user's* media of the same numeric id. A malformed attachment is dropped by VK silently — the post succeeds without it — so malformed entries are skipped here where the omission is logged
- Telegram degrades a rejected photo to a text post rather than failing: an unfetchable cover URL still leaves a publishable article. VK falls back to putting the URL in the message body when no attachment could be built, since otherwise the link would be unreachable
- Hashtags join multi-word phrases (`#станцияпыхтино`, not `#станция #пыхтино`): two generic tags compete with unrelated content where one specific tag does not
- Dzen's `_HEADING_REMAP` collapses `h1` to `h2` because Dzen renders the article title as `h1`, and a second one produces two competing headings on the page
- **The distribution worker is deliberately not wired**, per the task. All four adapters are complete and tested but nothing calls them, so an article still stops at `MEDIA_READY`. No live call has been made to any of the four platforms: everything is exercised against mocked transports, so real Telegram formatting, real VK attachment resolution, real OK signature acceptance and real Dzen publication are unverified
- Dzen's publishing API is partner-gated and its exact shape varies by agreement. The adapter implements the documented draft-then-publish flow against a configurable base URL, so pointing it at the real endpoint is configuration rather than a rewrite — but the payload field names are the part most likely to need adjustment against a live account

### Added — Package 4, Micro-step 4.1: WordPress master platform publisher

Work performed 2026-08-23, ~14:30 to ~16:00 (UTC+03:00).

- `app/services/wp_post_formatter.py` — the Jinja2 universal post template. `render_post()` wraps an article body with: the featured image as a `<figure>` with a real `<figcaption>` and `loading="eager"` (it is above the fold); a CSS scroll-snap carousel for the remaining attachments, built without a shortcode so it survives a theme change and needs no plugin; VK video players reusing `build_video_embed` so the markup is identical wherever it appears; a Schema.org `NewsArticle` JSON-LD block; and a related-articles `<aside>` capped at three links. `sanitize_article_html()` strips executable markup, `build_news_article_schema()` builds the structured data, and both are exported for reuse
- `app/services/wp_publisher.py` — the REST adapter. `publish()` creates or updates a post in one request with its taxonomy, `featured_media` and Rank Math meta already resolved. `resolve_tags()` walks three tiers — local `wp_tags_cache`, then a WordPress term search, then creation — writing every id it learns back to the cache. `resolve_category()` matches on **slug** rather than display name, so an editor renaming a category in the admin does not cause the wrong term to be attached. `build_rank_math_meta()` assembles `rank_math_title`, `rank_math_description`, `rank_math_focus_keyword` and `rank_math_robots`
- `app/services/wp_media_service.py` — `build_basic_auth_header()` extracted and exported so the media service and the publisher assemble the Application Password in exactly one place
- `app/main.py` — the publisher client is released on shutdown
- `tests/test_wp_publisher.py` — 89 tests. The session double enforces the real `lower(tag_name)` uniqueness, which is what makes the lost-race path reachable

### Fixed — Package 4, Micro-step 4.1

- Three of my own test defects, each of which had been asserting the wrong thing:
  - The JSON-LD breakout test probed the **headline**, where `_plain_text` already strips `</script>` as if it were a tag — so the test passed for the wrong reason and the `</` escape was never exercised. It now probes `keywords`, which is copied verbatim from model output and is the field where the escape is the only defence. Verified directly: a raw `</script>` in `keywords` is emitted as `<\/script>` and the JSON still parses back to the original text
  - `Settings` is a frozen Pydantic model, so `monkeypatch.setattr` on `settings.wp_base_url` raised `ValidationError`. The URL resolver is patched instead
  - The concurrent-cache-write test seeded the cache with the row it was meant to race against, which made the cache *hit* and skipped the WordPress path entirely — the lost-race branch never ran. The double now models rows written by a competing worker separately from rows this session can read, which is the actual production interleaving

### Security — Package 4, Micro-step 4.1

- **JSON-LD is serialised with `json.dumps`, never templated.** A value containing `</script>` interpolated as text would close the element and hand the rest of the document to an attacker. The payload is built as a dict, dumped, and `</` is then escaped as `<\/` — valid JSON, and the documented way to embed a payload in a `<script>` element. Two tests cover it
- **Autoescaping is on and exactly three values are marked safe**, each pre-processed: the article body after `sanitize_article_html`, the video markup from our own builder, and the JSON-LD block. An unsanitised `| safe` is the one mistake in a templating layer that turns a content pipeline into stored XSS. Tests assert that a hostile related-article title and a hostile `alt` text are both escaped
- The body sanitiser removes `script`, `style`, `iframe`, `object`, `embed`, `noscript`, `template`, `form`, `input`, `button`, `link`, `meta` and `base` **with their subtrees** (stripping only tags would leave `alert('xss')` as visible prose), plus every `on*` handler, inline `style`, `javascript:`/`vbscript:`/`data:` URL and HTML comment. This is defence in depth: `validate_generation` already *rejects* such a draft, but a body can reach the formatter from a manual dashboard edit that never passed the generator
- A timed-out write is **not** retried. WordPress may have created the post with only the response lost, and a blind retry would publish a duplicate — the one failure a news site cannot tolerate. Only 429 and 5xx are retried; 401/403 are terminal
- Optional fields are omitted rather than sent empty. WordPress reads `featured_media: 0` as "remove the featured image" and an empty `categories` array as "clear the categories", so placeholders on an *update* would silently strip what a previous publish set
- A non-HTTPS `WP_BASE_URL` is rejected in production, and the Application Password never appears in a log line or exception message (asserted)

- `pytest tests/` — **583 passed** (89 publisher, 69 wp media/vk, 66 media, 48 fixer, 112 content, 33 worker, 83 ingestion, 83 dedup), no external services

- `mypy --strict app/services/wp_post_formatter.py app/services/wp_publisher.py` — success
- `ruff check .` — all checks passed; `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` — success, 44 source files

### Notes — Package 4, Micro-step 4.1

- Publishing is idempotent through `wp_post_id`: an article that already carries one is **updated**, never re-created. Duplicate live articles are what search engines penalise, so this is the guarantee the whole method is built around
- The tag cache is the reason this module needs a database session. Ten articles about one event would otherwise issue ten identical term searches and race each other into creating duplicate terms; the `TagResolution` counters (`from_cache` / `from_wordpress` / `created`) are exposed so the dashboard can show whether the cache is earning its keep
- A lost cache race is resolved rather than raised. The insert runs inside a `SAVEPOINT`, so an `IntegrityError` from a competing worker does not poison the outer transaction — without it, a redundant cache write would roll back and lose the article
- Rank Math writes are **verified**. The REST API silently discards `meta` keys not registered with `show_in_rest`, answering 200 with the post created and the SEO fields absent; that failure is invisible for weeks otherwise. A dropped field is recorded as a warning and the post is still published, because an article without meta descriptions is worth far more than no article
- Tags are capped at eight per post, and the related block at three links: more reads as keyword stuffing and as a link farm respectively, which is what the ranking algorithms penalise
- `StrictUndefined` is set on the Jinja environment. A silently-undefined variable in a publishing template produces a page missing its featured image or its structured data with no error anywhere, which is the hardest failure to notice; a typo now raises during a test run
- The author is declared as an `Organization`, not a `Person`. The articles are produced by an automated newsroom, and claiming a human byline would be a false statement in structured data
- **No worker calls the publisher yet.** The service is complete and tested but not wired into the pipeline — an article still stops at `MEDIA_READY`. The publishing worker, the infinite-scroll theme snippet and the social adapters remain, and no live WordPress call has been made: everything is exercised against a mocked transport, so real Rank Math persistence and real term creation are unverified

### Added — Package 3, Micro-step 3.4: VK Video service, WP Media uploader & pipeline orchestrator

Work performed 2026-08-23, ~13:55 to ~15:30 (UTC+03:00). **Completes Package 3.**

- `app/services/vk_video_service.py` — async VK API client for the three-step video upload. `video_save(name, description, group_id)` reserves a slot and returns VK's raw payload; `save_video()` returns it parsed; `upload_video_file(upload_url, video_bytes, filename)` POSTs the multipart body; `upload()` runs the whole sequence. `build_video_embed()` produces responsive iframe markup for the Jinja2 template — a padding-ratio box rather than fixed pixels, with the intrinsic ratio derived from the requested dimensions so a vertical clip is not letterboxed. VK error codes are mapped onto typed exceptions (`VKAuthError`, `VKPermissionError`, `VKRateLimitError`, `VKUploadError`, `VKResponseError`) so callers can distinguish "retry later" from "stop and alert an operator"
- `app/services/wp_media_service.py` — async WordPress REST client. `transliterate()` / `slugify()` / `slugify_filename()` convert Cyrillic filenames to SEO slugs (`новости_дня.jpg` → `novosti-dnya.jpg`), following GOST 7.79-2000 System B so digraphs are spelled out rather than approximated. `upload_image()` sends the binary with `alt_text`, `title` and `caption` in one multipart request and returns `media_id`, `source_url` and `thumbnail_url`. Authentication is an Application Password over Basic auth, with the spaces WordPress prints for legibility stripped before encoding
- `app/workers/content_worker.py` — the orchestrator, and the second of only two modules that write to the database. Claims a cluster with no article, generates the draft in its editorial category, reviews and repairs it, embeds the final text into `articles.final_embedding`, processes media (scan → crop or regenerate → upload to WordPress), hosts any video on VK and injects the player after the first paragraph, then persists the `Article` at `MEDIA_READY`. Runs as its own ARQ worker on a cron offset from ingestion
- `app/main.py` — the VK and WordPress clients are released on shutdown alongside the others
- `tests/test_wp_media.py` — 69 tests covering transliteration, alt-text construction, the WP upload transport and the full VK flow
- `tests/test_content_worker.py` — 33 tests covering the orchestration: stage ordering, media strategy resolution, failure containment and persistence

### Fixed — Package 3, Micro-step 3.4

- Two tests in `tests/test_wp_media.py` shared the name `test_a_rate_limit_is_retried_then_raised`, so the WordPress one was shadowed by the VK one and **never ran**. Caught by ruff's `F811`, not by pytest, which reports a shadowed test as neither failing nor skipped. Both are now named for the service they exercise
- The first version of the worker test double stubbed `RawNews.source` with a plain object. Assigning to a relationship fires SQLAlchemy's backref event, which requires a mapped instance, so all 27 pipeline tests failed with `'_Source' object has no attribute '_sa_instance_state'`. The double is now a transient `Source`, which is legal because nothing is persisted

### Security — Package 3, Micro-step 3.4

- **VK reports application errors with HTTP 200.** Every response is inspected for an `error` object before its payload is read; a client that only checked `status_code` would treat a revoked token as success and then upload to a URL that does not exist
- The VK access token is sent as a form field, never in the query string, so it does not land in an intermediary's access log. The upload request carries **no** `Authorization` header at all: the reserved URL is pre-authorised, and sending the token to a third-party upload host would leak it. Both properties are asserted by tests
- `video.save` is called with `wallpost=0`. Letting it post as a side effect would produce a duplicate, unformatted wall entry alongside the one Package 4's adapter will publish
- A non-HTTPS `WP_BASE_URL` is rejected in production: Basic auth transmits the Application Password on every request, so plain HTTP would publish it to every hop
- A timed-out media upload is **not** retried. The request may have succeeded server-side with only the response lost, and a blind retry would create a second attachment while orphaning the first
- A rejected VK upload URL is not retried either: it is single-use, so recovery means a fresh `video.save` rather than burning the retry budget on a consumed URL
- Neither the VK token nor the WordPress Application Password appears in any log line or exception message (asserted by tests)
- The strictest media strategy across a cluster's sources wins. A cluster aggregates posts from several channels whose settings can disagree; honouring the most permissive one would republish exactly the media an operator disabled on another source

- `pytest tests/test_content_worker.py tests/test_wp_media.py` — 102 passed
- `pytest tests/` — **494 passed** (33 worker, 69 wp/vk, 66 media, 48 fixer, 112 content, 83 ingestion, 83 deduplication), no external services
- `ruff check .` — all checks passed
- `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` — success, no issues found in 42 source files

### Notes — Package 3, Micro-step 3.4

- **Two deliberate divergences from the task specification**, both to avoid corrupting the existing schema:
  - The spec asks for cluster status `PENDING_GENERATION` and article status `READY_TO_PUBLISH`. Neither exists in `ArticleStatus`, and per docs/01_DATA_PIPELINE.md the state after media processing is `MEDIA_READY`. Adding synonyms would put two names for one state into the enum, the migration and the publisher. "Pending generation" is expressed as a `NOT EXISTS` check for an article rather than as a flag, which is also what makes the step idempotent
  - The spec names `WP_API_URL` / `WP_USERNAME` / `WP_APP_PASSWORD`; the existing config defines `WP_BASE_URL` / `WP_APP_USER` / `WP_APP_PASSWORD`. The existing names are used, since renaming them would break `.env.example` and Package 1's validated settings
- Text failures abort an article; media failures do not. A cluster whose text cannot be generated has nothing to publish. A cluster whose *cover* failed still has a publishable article — WordPress renders a post without a featured image — so media errors are recorded on the row and in the run report, and the article is saved. Losing generated text because a CDN was briefly down would be the wrong trade
- Generation is fenced twice: a global run lock stops overlapping cron ticks, and a per-cluster lock stops the dashboard's manual trigger racing the scheduled run. Both skip on contention rather than queueing, because the scheduler fires on a fixed period and a queued run would arrive after the work was already done
- `url_slug` carries the cluster id's first four bytes. Two clusters covering one event produce near-identical titles, and the column is unique, so without a discriminator the second insert would fail on an article that is otherwise fine
- Only the first usable photo and the first video are processed. A Telegram album can carry ten items; the theme renders one cover and one player, so scanning the rest would spend OCR time and transfer cost on media nobody sees
- Telegram media descriptors store a file id rather than a URL, so this worker cannot fetch them — that download belongs to the GramJS bridge. Such entries are skipped with a recorded warning rather than failing the article
- The content worker is a **separate ARQ worker** from ingestion. Ingestion is bounded by Chromium's memory and content generation by the AI budget, so they need different concurrency; sharing a process would let a long Playwright scrape delay an article that is ready to be written
- Inpainting (`ROADMAP` Module 3.2) is deliberately deferred. The watermark path already covers the two outcomes that matter — crop a light badge, regenerate a heavy one — and inpainting is a quality refinement on the middle ground rather than a gap in the pipeline
- No live VK or WordPress call has been made. Both clients are exercised entirely against mocked transports, so real API behaviour, real VK transcoding and a real WordPress media library are unverified. The orchestrator's collaborators are all patched at its own module boundary, so what is tested is the wiring and the policy, not the services underneath — those have their own suites

### Added — Package 3, Micro-step 3.3: OCR watermark detector & Fal AI Flux generator

Work performed 2026-08-23, ~13:05 to ~14:30 (UTC+03:00).

- `app/services/watermark_detector.py` — `detect_watermarks(image_bytes)` returning `has_watermark`, `coverage_ratio`, `detected_text`, `needs_ai_generation`, plus a per-zone breakdown, `critical_text`, `is_croppable` and the source dimensions. Five zones are scanned — the four corners and the bottom-centre caption strip — because that is where channel branding is placed; a full-frame scan would be slower and would flag legitimate text inside the photo (a shop sign, a protest banner) as a watermark. Two independent signals per zone: Tesseract text matched against watermark-shaped patterns (`@handle`, `t.me/`, bare domains, copyright strings), and a high-contrast overlay plate detected from edge density plus bimodal brightness, so a logo OCR cannot read is still caught. At or below 15% coverage with no channel handle the image is croppable; above it, or with a handle anywhere, `needs_ai_generation` is set. OpenCV and Tesseract run via `asyncio.to_thread` per the strict-asyncio rule
- `crop_watermarks()` / `crop_watermarks_async()` — cuts the contaminated horizontal band, using the shallower strip depth when only the caption bar is dirty. Refuses rather than returning unchanged bytes when the image is clean, too contaminated, or branded in both bands
- `app/services/flux_generator.py` — `generate_flux_image(prompt, aspect_ratio="16:9")` returning image bytes from `fal-ai/flux/schnell`. Handles both Fal response shapes (immediate result and queue handle, the latter polled with a bounded budget), downloads the result from the CDN rather than storing the expiring URL, validates the payload is a plausible image under a size ceiling, and maps failures onto typed errors (`FluxNotConfiguredError`, `FluxAuthError`, `FluxRateLimitError`, `FluxTimeoutError`, `FluxGenerationError`). Six aspect ratios are supported, all multiples of 16 as Flux requires
- `app/main.py`, `app/workers/ingestion_worker.py` — the Fal HTTP client is released on shutdown alongside the other long-lived clients
- `tests/test_media_pipeline.py` — 66 tests. Images are synthesised in-process with OpenCV; Fal is served by `respx`

### Fixed — Package 3, Micro-step 3.3

- The first cut of the detector missed real watermarks entirely. Three separate defects, all found by running it against synthetic plates rather than by reading the code:
  - **OCR could not read the badge.** The zone was thresholded and only then upscaled, which magnifies a jagged binary mask. Upscaling the *greyscale* first keeps the anti-aliased stroke edges that Otsu then separates cleanly, and turns an unreadable zone into `Moscow_news`
  - **The mask thresholds were calibrated by intuition, not measurement.** The edge-density floor was set at 0.055 and the bimodality floor at 0.72; a rendered plate actually measures ~0.033 and ~0.28 against a blurred photo's ~0.000. Both floors now sit between the measured populations
  - **The `@` in a handle is rarely read as `@`.** Tesseract renders it as `y`, `u`, `w`, `o`, `c`, `e` or `©` depending on font and contrast, so a pattern requiring a literal `@` missed most real watermarks. The prefix class was widened and the discrimination moved to the trailing shape (an underscore or digit inside a lowercase run), which costs no precision
- Coverage was summed from raw zone areas, but the bottom-centre strip overlaps both bottom corners: a single caption bar reported ~23% and would have condemned a salvageable image. Coverage is now measured with a boolean mask

### Security — Package 3, Micro-step 3.3

- A downloaded image is rejected above a 24 MB ceiling and when its `Content-Type` is not `image/*`. The target VPS has 4 GB of RAM, so a hostile or mistaken response must not be pulled into memory
- `FAL_KEY` is sent as a header and never logged or interpolated into an exception message (asserted by a test)
- A missing `FAL_KEY` raises rather than degrading: unlike deduplication there is no zero-cost fallback for "produce an original photograph", and a silently missing cover would surface as a broken article
- Fal's safety checker is disabled deliberately. It rejects a noticeable share of ordinary news imagery — crowds, accidents, protests — and returns a blank; the prompt is built from our own already-validated article text, so the check costs covers without protecting anything

- `pytest tests/test_media_pipeline.py` — 66 passed
- `pytest` (full suite) — 392 passed (66 media, 48 fixer, 112 content, 83 ingestion, 83 deduplication), no external services
- `ruff check app/services/watermark_detector.py app/services/flux_generator.py` — all checks passed
- `mypy --strict app/services/watermark_detector.py app/services/flux_generator.py` — success
- `ruff check .` — all checks passed; `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` — success, 39 source files
- Environment confirmed: OpenCV 4.10.0, Tesseract 5.5.3 with `rus`+`eng` present

### Notes — Package 3, Micro-step 3.3

- The synthetic test photos are *blurred noise*, not flat colour. A flat background has almost no edges, so a plate would be detected against it trivially and the mask heuristic would appear to work without being tested; blurred noise has photographic texture, which is what the detector must discriminate against. One test asserts precisely that a busy photographic corner is *not* flagged
- Named-entity OCR patterns are matched against both binarisation polarities, because a watermark may be light-on-dark or dark-on-light and Otsu picks only one
- A missing Tesseract binary degrades to mask-only detection rather than failing the image; the text-detection tests skip in that environment, the mask tests run everywhere. The configured `TESSERACT_CMD` is honoured when it exists and otherwise the binary is left to `PATH`, because the container installs it at `/usr/bin` while a developer machine typically has `/usr/local/bin`
- Flux Schnell is called with 4 inference steps: it is distilled for very few steps and more is billed without improving output
- Not yet wired into the pipeline: nothing calls either service from a worker. The media worker that fetches a post's images, runs detection, decides crop-versus-regenerate and uploads the result is the next micro-step. Inpainting, VK Video and the WordPress media uploader remain untouched
- No live Fal AI call has been made; the client is exercised entirely against a mocked transport, so real API behaviour and real cover quality are unverified

### Added — Package 3, Micro-step 3.2: Fixer/QA sub-agent & article embedding generator

Work performed 2026-08-23, ~13:20 to ~14:40 (UTC+03:00).

- `app/services/content_fixer.py` — the QA gate between generation and publication. `review_and_fix_article(raw_sources_text, generated_article, model_name=None)` runs a **free** review first: verifiable facts (dates, sums, percentages, counts) are extracted from the sources and from the draft with `extract_facts` and diffed, markup structure is checked for `<h2>`/`<p>`/`<ul>`, `title`/`seo_title`/`seo_description` are checked against their limits, headline-versus-body coherence is checked by stem overlap, and residual AI clichés are reported. A clean draft costs **zero tokens** and is returned untouched. Only when defects are found is the fast model (`AI_MODEL_SEO`) asked to repair the *enumerated* defects; the repair is re-reviewed and accepted only if it is genuinely better. `review_article()` is exposed separately so the dashboard can show a verdict without spending anything
- `app/services/embedding_service.py` — `get_text_embedding(text)` returning a schema-width (1536) vector for `articles.final_embedding` and `news_clusters.embedding`. Redis cache with a 24-hour TTL, keys scoped by `AI_MODEL_EMBEDDING`; `embed_batch` sends one request for many texts and collapses duplicates within a batch; an outer retry with escalating backoff handles a quota exhausted mid-run after the client's own retries are spent
- `app/main.py`, `app/workers/ingestion_worker.py` — the embedding cache client is released on shutdown alongside the other long-lived resources
- `tests/test_content_fixer.py` — 48 tests against `respx` and `fakeredis`: invented sums/dates/percentages, tolerated versus excessive omission, structure and metadata violations, the repair flow including a malformed repair and a repair that does not help, cache hits, TTL, model-scoped keys, corrupt and wrong-width cache entries, Redis outage, batching, and rate-limit recovery

### Fixed — Package 3, Micro-step 3.2

- The headline/body coherence check reported a false mismatch on a *correct* draft: it compared whole words, so a headline's "станция" did not match the body's "станцию". Russian inflection made the check fire on sound copy, which would have sent good articles to a paid repair. Words are now compared as crude five-character stems

### Security — Package 3, Micro-step 3.2

- Fabricated figures are treated as fatal at any quantity: a number, sum, percentage or date in the draft that appears in no source is a fabrication, and a well-formed article with an invented figure is more dangerous than a malformed one. Omission is scored separately and tolerated up to `MAX_DROPPED_FACT_RATIO`, because condensing a wire story is legitimate editing
- A failed repair returns the original draft with its QA report attached rather than a partially applied fix. A caller can only decide whether to publish, queue for a human or discard if it still has a draft whose defects are known
- Embedding cache entries always carry a TTL. Redis runs with `maxmemory-policy noeviction` because it is the ARQ broker and the LSH index, so an entry written without an expiry would eventually make the instance refuse writes and take the queue down with it
- An empty text is refused rather than embedded: a degenerate vector in the HNSW index would match every query it reached

- `pytest tests/test_content_fixer.py` — 48 passed
- `pytest` (full suite) — 326 passed (48 fixer, 112 content, 83 ingestion, 83 deduplication), no external services
- `ruff check app/services/content_fixer.py app/services/embedding_service.py` — all checks passed
- `mypy --strict app/services/content_fixer.py app/services/embedding_service.py` — success
- `ruff check .` — all checks passed; `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` — success, 37 source files

### Notes — Package 3, Micro-step 3.2

- Fact extraction is shared with the deduplicator deliberately. Level 3 of dedup decides whether a post carries *new* facts; the fixer decides whether a draft *dropped or invented* facts. Two independent extractors would drift, leaving the pipeline with two different definitions of a fact — dedup merging on one while QA rejects on another
- Named entities are excluded from the fidelity comparison. The extractor's capitalised-bigram heuristic also picks up sentence openers and agency names that an edited article legitimately drops, so requiring them would fire constantly on good copy
- Bare integers at or below 100 are exempt from the invention check: "три станции" is prose, not a statistic. Money, percentages and dates are checked at every magnitude because those are always claims
- The fixer prompt is framed as copy-editing and explicitly forbids rewriting. An open rewrite would reintroduce the clichés the generator was forced to avoid and could drift further from the sources
- Long texts are embedded but not cached: an entry costs ~25 KB regardless of input size, and a one-off article body will never be read twice. Short repeated strings — titles, headlines — are what the cache is for
- Still open in Module 3.1/3.2: no live LLM or embedding call has been made; both services are exercised entirely against mocked transports, so real-model behaviour is unverified. Writing `final_embedding` into `articles` is a worker concern and is not wired up yet

### Notes — Package 3, Micro-step 3.1

- `CATEGORIES` slugs are persisted to `articles.category` and used as WordPress category slugs, so they are a stable contract: renaming one orphans every article already filed under it
- The anti-slop rules are restated inside every system prompt as well as enforced after generation. Instructing up front is far cheaper than a retry, and the filter is what makes the instruction binding
- Soft slop is repaired in place rather than regenerated: spending a full article's tokens to delete "стоит отметить, что" would be indefensible
- An unknown `category_slug` in a reply is corrected to the requested category rather than rejected — the prose may be fine, and the caller already knows what it asked for
- Not yet implemented in Module 3.1: the Fixer/QA sub-agent and the embedding generator for `articles.final_embedding`. No live LLM call has been made; the engine is exercised entirely against mocked transports

## [1.1.0] — 2026-08-23 — Package 2: Ingestion Engine & Zero-Cost Deduplication

Work performed 2026-08-22 ~21:00 to 2026-08-23 ~11:30 (UTC+03:00).

### Added

Ingestion services:

- `app/services/telegram_ingestor.py` — GramJS bridge client over a single reusable `httpx.AsyncClient`: `/dialogs`, `/messages`, `/messages/read`; pagination with deliberate `min_id = max_id_seen - INGEST_OVERLAP_MESSAGES` overlap so edited posts and late media are re-seen; album grouping by `groupedId` with the lowest message id as the stable key and the longest caption as the text; media metadata only (no binary downloads); retries with exponential backoff and jitter on timeouts, network errors and 5xx, `Retry-After` honoured on 429, no retry on other 4xx
- `app/services/text_utils.py` — 0-token heuristic filter: Telegram `entities` → HTML converted over **UTF-16 code units** (Python string offsets would corrupt every post containing an emoji), overlapping/nested entities resolved into correctly nested HTML, digest detection (numbered lists, bullet runs, summary headlines), advertising detection (`erid`, promo codes, referral/bot links, shorteners, link density), machine-readable `REASON_*` identifiers persisted to `raw_news.filter_reasons`
- `app/services/web_scraper.py` — Playwright crawler: process-wide `BrowserPool` singleton with double-checked locking, page concurrency bounded by a semaphore (`PLAYWRIGHT_MAX_CONCURRENT_PAGES`, default 2) and a throwaway `BrowserContext` per scrape; resource blocking for images/fonts/stylesheets/media plus known analytics and ad hosts; stealth via optional `playwright-stealth` with a built-in init-script fallback; two-stage extraction (CSS/XPath rules, then Trafilatura off the event loop via `asyncio.to_thread`); Russian-language date parsing (ISO, dotted, slashed, epoch, month names) anchored to the project timezone; `discover_links` and the read-only `test_crawl_url` preview
- `app/services/deduplicator.py` — three-level engine: level 1 MinHash LSH in Redis (word 3-gram shingles, banded buckets, signatures cached next to the buckets so a crowded bucket costs two round trips and PostgreSQL is never touched), level 2 pgvector cosine search over recent clusters with an LLM arbiter for the middle band, level 3 novelty check comparing canonical facts (dates, quantities, money, percentages, multi-word entities). Conservative default: a disabled, failed or unparseable arbiter opens a new cluster rather than merging two possibly distinct events
- `app/services/ai_client.py` — OpenRouter client for embeddings and the YES/NO arbiter, degrading gracefully when unconfigured

Orchestration and API:

- `app/workers/ingestion_worker.py` — ARQ worker and cron job (every five minutes): global run lock plus one lock per source, both acquired non-blockingly and skipped when held; one transaction per source so a late failure cannot roll back earlier sources; `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT (source_id, external_post_id) DO NOTHING ... RETURNING id` for idempotency; filtered posts stored terminal and deliberately kept out of the LSH index; `last_scraped_at` updated only on success; per-source and aggregate run reports
- `app/workers/redis_lock.py` — token-fenced Redis lock released via a Lua compare-and-delete
- `app/api/ingestion.py` — admin endpoints: Telegram/website source upserts (`ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE`, so resubmitting a form converges on one row), selector dry-run, synchronous manual trigger reporting a held lock as 409, source listing, Telegram dialog picker, soft delete
- `app/schemas/ingestion.py` — Pydantic v2 request/response contracts with strict validation at the edge
- `migrations/versions/20260822_2100-0002_ingestion_dedup_fields.py` — `dedup_status` enum, `is_ad`, `filter_reasons`, `similarity_score`, `dedup_level`

Tests (166, no external services — `respx` for the bridge, a loopback `http.server` for the crawler, `fakeredis`, a session double for PostgreSQL):

- `tests/test_ingestion.py` (83) — bridge transport and retries, token never leaked into errors, album grouping, UTF-16 entity conversion, filter heuristics, browser-pool singleton and page bound, resource blocking verified by what the fixture server was never asked for, rules/Trafilatura/hybrid strategies, `content_html` contract, link discovery, preview warnings, permit release on failure
- `tests/test_deduplicator.py` (83) — shingling and signature round-trips, LSH bucketing and TTLs, threshold ladder, arbiter behaviour, fact extraction and delta merging, idempotent replay, graceful degradation without AI

### Fixed

- `ScrapedArticle.content_html` was declared on the dataclass but never passed to the constructor, so **every** `scrape_article()` call raised `TypeError: missing 1 required positional argument`. The website ingestion path was completely non-functional. Both extraction stages now produce it: the rules path reads the container's `inner_html`, the Trafilatura path renders the library's extraction tree through `build_html_output` (switched from `bare_extraction(as_dict=True)`, which discards that tree and also emitted a `PendingDeprecationWarning`), and a body that yields no markup is wrapped in escaped `<p>` paragraphs
- `raw_news.clean_html` for website sources stored escaped plain text, losing the article's paragraphs, headings and links. It now receives the sanitised article HTML, and the heuristic filter reads that same fragment so markup and plain text can never describe different content
- `_sanitize_html` initially re-emitted a fragment's leading text node verbatim; a body starting with `<script>` would have been re-serialised into a live tag, defeating the sanitiser. Own text nodes are now escaped on serialisation. Caught by the hostile-page test
- A content container of bare text nodes sanitised to tagless text, which would have been stored as `clean_html` and rendered as one run-on block. Such a body is now reported as markup-less so the paragraph-wrapping fallback applies

### Security

- Article HTML is treated as hostile input at the single choke point every stored fragment passes through: `script`, `style`, `iframe`, `noscript`, `object`, `embed` and `template` are dropped **with their subtrees** (stripping only the tags would leave `alert('xss')` as visible prose), every `on*` handler and `style` attribute is removed, and URL attributes carrying `javascript:`, `vbscript:` or `data:` are dropped after control characters are stripped so `java&#9;script:` cannot slip through. An unparseable fragment yields `""` rather than being passed along
- The ingestion API is fenced by an interim shared-secret bearer guard compared with `secrets.compare_digest`, applied to the whole router including the read paths, since which feeds are watched is not public either. Package 5 replaces it with the JWT cookie session
- Non-http(s) crawl targets are rejected before a browser page is opened, so `file://` and `chrome://` cannot be reached through source configuration
- The GramJS token is never included in an exception message or log line (asserted by a test)
- Source deletion is a soft delete: `raw_news.source_id` cascades, so a hard delete would destroy the idempotency keys, the MinHash signatures and the filter audit trail

### Verification

- `pytest` — 166 passed, no external services required; the suite runs with `filterwarnings = ["error"]`, so a new deprecation or a leaked connection fails the run
- `ruff check .` — all checks passed
- `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` (strict) — success, no issues found in 32 source files

### Notes

- Level 1 never touches PostgreSQL by design: signatures are cached in Redis beside the bucket sets, which is what keeps the latency budget under a crowded bucket. They are still persisted to `raw_news.minhash_signature` so the index can be rebuilt after a Redis flush
- `MINHASH_SEED` is part of the stored signature format; changing it invalidates every persisted signature and must be treated as a migration
- Filtered digests and adverts are deliberately excluded from the LSH index: a digest shares shingles with every story it summarises and would start matching unrelated real news
- `DedupStatus.DUPLICATE` is never emitted; the spec's decision table resolves every confirmed duplicate through level 3 into `SKIPPED_DUPLICATE` or `UPDATE`
- Not exercised in this package: a live GramJS bridge and a real PostgreSQL/Redis pair. Level 2 runs against a session double, so pgvector's own HNSW behaviour is covered by the Package 1 smoke test rather than here

## [1.0.0] — 2026-08-22 — Package 1: Infrastructure, Docker, Async DB Engine, Models & Alembic Migrations

Work performed 2026-08-22, ~17:50 to ~20:40 (UTC+03:00).

### Added

Environment & tooling:

- `.env.example` — all environment variables: PostgreSQL (POSTGRES_USER/PASSWORD/DB/HOST/PORT, pool tuning), Redis (REDIS_PASSWORD, REDIS_URL, REDIS_MAXMEMORY, pool settings), security (SECRET_KEY, ALGORITHM=HS256, ACCESS_TOKEN_EXPIRE_MINUTES=1440), AI/API credentials (OPENROUTER_API_KEY, FAL_KEY, GRAMJS_API_URL, GRAMJS_TOKEN, VK_ACCESS_TOKEN, OK_ACCESS_TOKEN, OK_APPLICATION_KEY, OK_SESSION_SECRET_KEY, DZEN_TOKEN, TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN), WordPress (WP_BASE_URL, WP_APP_USER, WP_APP_PASSWORD), SSH (SSH_REMOTE_HOST, SSH_REMOTE_USER, SSH_REMOTE_PORT, SSH_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH), AI models & thresholds (SIMILARITY_LOWER_THRESHOLD=0.75, SIMILARITY_UPPER_THRESHOLD=0.92, MINHASH_NUM_PERM=128), Playwright, media/OCR, SEO analytics, and dev-only host port overrides
- `requirements.txt` — pinned dependencies including fastapi 0.115.6, uvicorn 0.34.0, gunicorn 23.0.0, sqlalchemy[asyncio] 2.0.36, asyncpg 0.30.0, alembic 1.14.0, pgvector 0.3.6, pydantic 2.10.4, pydantic-settings 2.7.0, redis[hiredis] 5.2.1, arq 0.26.3, httpx 0.28.1, jinja2 3.1.5, python-multipart 0.0.20, bcrypt 4.2.1, pyjwt 2.10.1, playwright 1.49.1, trafilatura 2.0.0, opencv-python-headless 4.10.0.84, pytesseract 0.3.13, numpy 2.2.1, pillow 11.1.0, datasketch 1.6.5, asyncssh 2.19.0, python-json-logger 3.2.1
- `pyproject.toml` — ruff (line-length 90, target py312) and mypy strict configuration
- `.gitignore`, `.dockerignore` — exclude secrets, virtualenvs, caches and runtime artifacts from VCS and build context
- `README.md` — setup, dev/prod deployment, database, verification and health-probe documentation

Docker infrastructure:

- `Dockerfile` — multi-stage build on python:3.12-slim-bookworm. Builder stage compiles dependencies into `/opt/venv`; runtime stage installs tesseract-ocr with rus+eng language packs, OpenCV runtime libraries (libgl1, libglx-mesa0, libglib2.0-0), curl, git, openssh-client and tini, installs Playwright Chromium via `playwright install --with-deps chromium`, creates non-root user `svodka` (uid/gid 1000), sets workdir `/app`, and declares a liveness HEALTHCHECK against `/health`
- `docker-compose.yml` — base composition: `postgres` (pgvector/pgvector:pg16, named volume `postgres_data`, `pg_isready` healthcheck), `redis` (redis:7-alpine, named volume `redis_data`, authenticated `redis-cli ping` healthcheck, `--requirepass`, `maxmemory-policy noeviction`), `app` (builds ./Dockerfile, depends on both healthchecks, named log/media volumes, uvicorn)
- `docker-compose.override.yml` — development overlay: publishes PostgreSQL, Redis and the app on 127.0.0.1 only with `.env`-configurable host ports, mounts `./:/app`, enables `--reload`
- `docker-compose.prod.yml` — production overlay: no datastore host exposure, app bound to 127.0.0.1, gunicorn with uvicorn workers, tuned PostgreSQL settings (max_connections=60, work_mem=8MB, shared_buffers=384MB), Redis persistence with copy-on-write headroom, json-file log rotation, memory limits sized for a 4 GB VPS (postgres 1.5G, redis 768M, app 1.5G)

Core application:

- `app/__init__.py` — package version
- `app/config.py` — Pydantic v2 `BaseSettings` reading `.env`, frozen and cached; builds the async DSN via SQLAlchemy `URL.create`; exposes `database_url_safe` with the password masked for logging; validators enforce similarity-threshold ordering, embedding-dimension/schema agreement, and rejection of placeholder or short secrets when `APP_ENV=production`
- `app/database.py` — async engine via `create_async_engine` with asyncpg, bounded pool with pre-ping and recycling, `AsyncSessionLocal` sessionmaker, declarative `Base` with a naming convention, `get_db()` dependency that commits on success and rolls back on error, `check_database_connection()`, `dispose_engine()`
- `app/redis_client.py` — shared `BlockingConnectionPool` (applies backpressure instead of erroring on exhaustion), `get_redis()`, `check_redis_connection()`, `close_redis_pool()`
- `app/main.py` — FastAPI app with lifespan that aborts startup when PostgreSQL is unreachable and logs a warning when Redis is unreachable; `GET /health` liveness probe (PostgreSQL only, so a Redis outage does not mark a serving container unhealthy); `GET /health/ready` readiness probe (PostgreSQL + Redis); dependency errors logged server-side, not returned to callers

SQLAlchemy 2.0 models (`app/models/`):

- `user.py` — `User` (id, username unique, password_hash, is_active, created_at)
- `source.py` — `Source` (id, source_type, name, identifier, is_active, skip_media, media_strategy, crawl_rules, last_scraped_at, created_at), unique on (source_type, identifier)
- `raw_news.py` — `RawNews` (id, source_id, external_post_id, post_url, raw_text, clean_html, entities, media_data, minhash_signature, is_digest, is_processed, cluster_id, created_at), unique on (source_id, external_post_id), GIN index on entities, partial index on the unprocessed backlog
- `cluster.py` — `NewsCluster` (id UUID via `uuid_generate_v4()`, title, category, risk_level, enrichment_data, embedding `vector(1536)`, is_published, created_at), HNSW cosine index, partial index on the unpublished backlog
- `article.py` — `Article` (id, cluster_id, category, wp_category_id, title, content_html, seo_title, seo_description, seo_keywords, wp_tag_ids, url_slug, wp_post_id, wp_image_id, wp_image_ids, video_links, final_embedding `vector(1536)`, status, platform_statuses, error_message, retry_count, published_at, created_at, updated_at), HNSW cosine index, GIN index on platform_statuses, composite (status, created_at) index
- `tag_cache.py` — `WPTagCache` (id, tag_name, wp_id) with a functional unique index on `lower(tag_name)`
- `setting.py` — `SystemSetting` (key PK, value JSONB, description, updated_at)
- `enums.py` — `SourceType`, `MediaStrategy`, `RiskLevel`, `ArticleStatus`, `Platform`, `PlatformStatus`; `pg_enum()` helper persisting lowercase enum *values* as native PostgreSQL labels; `default_platform_statuses()`
- `types.py` — `MutableJSONB` / `MutableJSONBList` mutation-tracking JSONB types propagating nested in-place edits to the owning instance, required for retry idempotency
- `vector.py` — single source of truth for `EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` (1536), HNSW parameters and the cosine operator class; `embedding_column()` returns a deferred vector column
- `__init__.py` — model registry exporting all models and enums

Alembic async migrations:

- `alembic.ini` — no credentials stored; timestamped revision filenames
- `migrations/env.py` — async environment importing `Base.metadata` from `app.models`; passes the DSN directly to `create_async_engine` rather than through `alembic.ini`, so percent-encoded characters in a password cannot be misread as configparser interpolation
- `migrations/script.py.mako` — revision template with pgvector imports and modern typing
- `migrations/versions/20260822_1505-0001_initial_schema_initial_schema.py` — initial migration: creates `uuid-ossp` and `vector` extensions; four native enums (source_type, media_strategy, risk_level, article_status); seven tables (users, sources, news_clusters, raw_news, articles, wp_tags_cache, system_settings); HNSW cosine indexes on both embedding columns; GIN indexes; partial queue indexes; functional `lower(tag_name)` unique index; full downgrade path

Verification tooling:

- `scripts/__init__.py`, `scripts/smoke_test.py` — destructive end-to-end verification script guarded so it refuses to run when `APP_ENV=production`, when the database name lacks a `_verify`/`_test`/`_scratch`/`_ci` suffix, or when any table already holds rows

### Changed

A self-review pass was run after the initial implementation; the following corrections were applied:

- Database DSN construction moved from `PostgresDsn.build` to SQLAlchemy `URL.create`, because the former did not escape credentials and a password containing `@` or `/` corrupted the DSN so asyncpg misparsed the host
- Alembic no longer routes the DSN through `alembic.ini`, which previously aborted every Alembic command with `ValueError: invalid interpolation syntax` when the password contained `%`
- JSONB columns (`platform_statuses`, `enrichment_data`, `entities`, `media_data`, `crawl_rules`, `video_links`) converted to mutation-tracking types so in-place nested edits are flushed; previously a retry would re-read stale state and could republish
- `/health` split into a PostgreSQL-only liveness probe and a new `/health/ready` readiness probe, so a Redis outage no longer marks a serving container unhealthy
- Redis switched from `allkeys-lru` to `noeviction` (it is the ARQ broker and MinHash LSH index, not a cache) and given `--requirepass`; the client pool switched to `BlockingConnectionPool`
- Development host ports moved from `0.0.0.0` to `127.0.0.1` and made configurable through `.env`
- Removed the redundant `ix_articles_status` and `ix_raw_news_source_id` indexes (already covered by the composite and unique indexes); converted the queue-polling indexes to partial indexes
- Embedding columns made deferred so list queries no longer transfer 1536-dimension vectors
- Embedding dimension consolidated into one constant validated against `AI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` at startup
- Compose no longer repeats `REDIS_URL`/`POSTGRES_HOST`/`POSTGRES_PORT` under `environment:`, which had silently overridden `.env`
- Production PostgreSQL and Redis memory settings reduced to fit inside their container limits, preventing OOM restart loops
- Removed dead code: `database_url_sync`, `Base.to_dict()`, and an unreachable Alembic `include_object` filter

### Security

- Production startup refuses to boot when `SECRET_KEY` or `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` is missing, still a placeholder, or (for the secret key) shorter than 32 characters
- Health endpoints no longer return raw backend exception text; connection details are logged server-side only
- PostgreSQL and Redis are not published to the host in production; development publishes to loopback only
- Redis requires authentication via `--requirepass`
- Container runs as non-root user uid 1000
- The destructive smoke test cannot run against a production or non-scratch database

### Verification

- `ruff check .` — all checks passed
- `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` (strict mode) — success, no issues found in 19 source files
- Migration roundtrip against PostgreSQL 16 + pgvector 0.8.0: `alembic upgrade head` -> `alembic downgrade base` -> `alembic upgrade head`; after downgrade only `alembic_version` remained, with no orphan tables or enum types
- `alembic check` — no new upgrade operations detected, confirming the ORM models and the migration do not drift
- Migration verified with the password `Str0ng@Pa%ss/w`, confirming special characters no longer break Alembic
- `scripts/smoke_test.py` passed every check: server-side defaults via Core inserts that omit the columns (`uuid_generate_v4()`, native enum defaults, the JSONB `platform_statuses` default matching `default_platform_statuses()`); pgvector cosine *ordering* across three distinct vectors (exact 0.00e+00, near 0.0000, opposite 2.0000); in-place JSONB mutation persistence across a commit/reload; deferred embeddings absent from a plain `select()` and loadable via `undefer()`; the `(source_id, external_post_id)` unique constraint blocking duplicate ingestion; the `lower(tag_name)` unique index blocking a case-variant tag; `ON DELETE CASCADE` on articles and `ON DELETE SET NULL` on raw_news.cluster_id
- Smoke-test guards verified: aborted on `APP_ENV=production`, aborted on a non-scratch database name, and aborted on a non-empty database without deleting the pre-existing row
- Production secret guards verified across five cases: placeholder secret key, 31-character secret key, placeholder database password, mismatched embedding dimension (all rejected) and a valid production configuration (accepted)
- Docker image built successfully; inside the container verified non-root uid 1000, workdir `/app`, Python 3.12.14, tesseract 5.3.0 with `eng`, `osd`, `rus`, OpenCV 4.10.0, git 2.39.5, all application dependencies importing, and Playwright Chromium 131.0.6778.33 launching and rendering a page
- Full stack via Docker Compose: postgres, redis and app all reported healthy, with `depends_on` health gating honoured; `alembic upgrade head` and `alembic check` ran inside the container; the smoke test passed inside the container and its guard correctly refused the real application database
- Degradation behaviour verified: with Redis stopped, `GET /health` returned 200 and the container stayed `healthy`, while `GET /health/ready` returned 503 with `status: degraded` and a redacted error; both recovered after Redis restarted
- Schema persistence verified across a PostgreSQL container restart via the named volume
- Both compose configurations validated with `docker compose config`; the production overlay publishes zero datastore ports

### Notes

- `libgl1-mesa-glx` was replaced with `libgl1` + `libglx-mesa0` because the former package was removed from Debian bookworm and would fail the build
- Redis is intentionally configured with `noeviction`; cache-like data must carry an explicit TTL
- Changing the embedding dimension requires an `ALTER TABLE` plus an index rebuild in a new migration

## [2.0.1] — 2026-08-23 — Production deployment to VPS behind Nginx Proxy Manager

Deployed the stack to `144.31.141.17` (Ubuntu 24.04, 6 vCPU, 9.7 GB RAM) and made it
serviceable behind an existing Nginx Proxy Manager instance at `150.241.98.133`.

Three genuine defects surfaced only under a real deployment, all now fixed and
covered by tests. Each was invisible to the 829-test suite because each lived in the
gap between the application and its operating environment.

### Fixed

- **ARQ workers stole each other's jobs.** All three workers polled ARQ's shared
  default queue, so whichever polled first claimed a job regardless of the functions
  it had registered. Observed in production as
  `job publisher_task:… function 'publisher_task' not found` logged by the
  *ingestion* worker — the publisher pass was consumed and lost, silently, on a
  worker that could not run it. Each `WorkerSettings` now declares its own
  `queue_name`.
- **Workers had no supervision.** They were documented to start with
  `docker exec -d app arq …`: invisible to `compose ps`, no restart policy, and gone
  for good on the next app restart, which stops the pipeline without stopping the
  site. They are now first-class compose services. Chromium is isolated in
  `worker-ingestion` (2 GB, `shm_size` 512 MB) instead of competing with gunicorn
  inside the app container's 1.5 GB cap.
- **Worker healthchecks could never pass.** Inherited from the image, they curl port
  8000, which a worker does not serve; every worker would have sat permanently
  `unhealthy`. Replaced with a Redis ping — a worker that cannot reach its queue is
  genuinely unable to work.

### Added

- `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOSTS` and `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS` settings, with
  `TrustedHostMiddleware` and optional CORS in `app/main.py`. `FORWARDED_ALLOW_IPS`
  is set from `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOSTS` in compose, because uvicorn applies the
  `X-Forwarded-*` rewrite itself before the app is entered; adding the same
  middleware in application code would apply it twice.
- `APP_BIND_ADDRESS`, for a reverse proxy running on a different host.
- Four pre-flight checks in `deploy_vps.sh`, each for a failure observed during this
  deployment: CIDR notation in `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOSTS`, loopback missing from
  `ALLOWED_HOSTS`, an unfiltered `0.0.0.0` binding, and worker services not running.
- `test_each_worker_polls_its_own_queue` plus five reverse-proxy configuration tests
  (835 total, up from 829).

### Security

- **A `ufw` rule does not protect a published Docker port.** Docker installs its own
  DNAT rules in the `nat` table, which are evaluated before ufw's `INPUT` chain. With
  `ufw allow from <proxy> to any port 8000` in place, the dashboard was confirmed
  reachable from an unrelated external address — HTTP 200. Port 8000 is now filtered
  in `DOCKER-USER`, the chain Docker consults, with rules persisted via
  `iptables-persistent`. The deploy script's check was corrected to inspect that
  chain; it had previously reported "restricted by ufw" while the port was open.
- `TRUSTED_PROXY_HOSTS='*'` and `CORS_ALLOW_ORIGINS='*'` are refused in production.
  Trusting `X-Forwarded-Proto` from any source lets a direct caller declare its
  plain-HTTP request secure and forge client IPs in the audit log.
- `ufw` enabled with default-deny inbound; SSH key authentication replaced password
  auth for all deployment commands.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` (strict) clean across 57
  source files; 835 tests pass in both fixed and randomised order
- Migrations applied on the host: 8 tables, `pgvector` and `uuid-ossp` present,
  schema at `0002_ingestion_dedup_fields`
- All six services report `healthy`; every cron schedule observed firing and
  completing on its own worker, with zero `function not found` occurrences after the
  fix
- Login verified end to end: wrong password redirects to `?error=1`, correct password
  issues a `HttpOnly; Secure; SameSite=lax` cookie, and all six dashboard pages
  (`/`, `/articles`, `/sources`, `/settings`, `/seo`, `/logs`) return 200
- Proxy handling verified both ways: `X-Forwarded-For` from a trusted source is
  honoured (client logged as `203.0.113.9`), and the same header from an untrusted
  container is ignored (logged as its real `172.18.0.8`)
- `Host: evil.test` is rejected with 400 while `127.0.0.1` still returns 200, so the
  container healthcheck and the deploy probe keep working
- External reachability confirmed blocked after the `DOCKER-USER` fix; loopback and
  the bridge network still reach the app
- Memory at rest, well inside every cap: app 251 MB / 1.5 GB, worker-ingestion
  165 MB / 2 GB, worker-content 92 MB / 1 GB, worker-publisher 64 MB / 1 GB,
  postgres 42 MB / 1.5 GB, redis 10 MB / 768 MB

### Deployment notes

- **`gunicorn` rejects CIDR in `forwarded_allow_ips`.** It resolves every entry with
  `ipaddress.ip_address`, unlike uvicorn's middleware, which accepts networks. A
  value of `172.16.0.0/12` therefore passes a local uvicorn test and then crash-loops
  the container with only `does not appear to be an IPv4 or IPv6 address` in the log.
  List individual addresses; the bridge gateway is `172.18.0.1`.
- **Rebuild after changing application code.** The Dockerfile `COPY`s the source, so
  `rsync` to the host updates the files without changing the running image. A config
  change that appeared to have no effect was traced to this.
- Nginx Proxy Manager host entry: forward to `144.31.141.17:8000`, scheme `http`, with
  `X-Forwarded-Proto` and `X-Forwarded-For` passed through, and websocket support on
  for the SSE log stream.
- Still required for public access: an A record for `svodkanews.team-big.ru`
  (currently unresolvable) and the proxy host entry itself.

## [2.0.2] — 2026-08-23 — Review fixes: host validation and worker liveness

A code review of the 2.0.1 deployment found five issues, all fixed here. The first
matters most: a security control that reported itself as enforced while being fully
disabled.

### Security

- **`ALLOWED_HOSTS` could silently disable Host validation.** Starlette sets
  `allow_any = "*" in allowed_hosts` and then skips the check entirely, and the
  production validator never inspected the field. `ALLOWED_HOSTS=127.0.0.1,*` booted
  cleanly, satisfied the deploy script's loopback *substring* check, and printed
  `ok ALLOWED_HOSTS=...` — while accepting every forged `Host`. Production now
  refuses to boot on three distinct forms, each verified against the real image:
  unset (the middleware is never registered), any bare `*` entry, and an allowlist
  omitting `127.0.0.1` (which would 400 the container healthcheck). A subdomain
  wildcard such as `*.example.com` remains valid.
- `deploy_vps.sh` performs the same three checks, matching `*` as a comma-delimited
  element rather than a substring, so it fails before replacing a running deployment.

### Fixed

- **Worker healthchecks could not detect a wedged worker.** The previous check
  spawned a separate process to ping Redis, which proves only that the container has
  network and credentials — a worker blocked in a hung Chromium page kept reporting
  `healthy`. Replaced with `arq <WorkerSettings> --check`, which reads the
  `<queue>:health-check` key the worker refreshes from inside its own main loop, and
  `health_check_interval` is set to 30s on all three workers. ARQ's 3600s default was
  observed live: the key's TTL was 3010s, so a stalled worker would have looked
  healthy for nearly an hour.
- The `ingestion_task` docstring documented
  `enqueue_job("ingestion_task", source_ids=[7])` with no `_queue_name`, which after
  the 2.0.1 queue split would land on `arq:queue` where no worker listens. No code
  used this path, but it encoded exactly the silent job-loss pattern the split was
  meant to remove. Corrected, along with a comment claiming the dashboard reaches the
  task by enqueue when it runs the pass inline.
- `test_the_host_allowlist_keeps_the_container_healthcheck_working` built its
  middleware from a hardcoded tuple, so it asserted Starlette's behaviour and passed
  regardless of configuration. Replaced with four tests driven from `Settings`, three
  of which fail if a guard is removed.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 57 source files; 841 tests pass
  in randomised order (up from 835)
- Worker liveness proven by freezing a worker: `docker pause` for 45s produced
  `Health check failed: no health check sentinel value found` and Docker marked the
  container `unhealthy`; after `docker unpause` it returned to `healthy` within 15s.
  Health-key TTLs confirmed at single-digit seconds instead of 3010
- All three unsafe `ALLOWED_HOSTS` forms rejected by the production image and by
  `deploy_vps.sh --check-only`; the seven-case wildcard matcher verified, including
  `*.example.com` passing and `127.0.0.1,*` failing
- Live re-verified after redeploy: `Host: evil.test` -> 400, `svodkanews.team-big.ru`
  and `127.0.0.1` -> 200, all six dashboard pages -> 200, external access to port
  8000 still blocked

## [2.0.3] — 2026-08-23 — Reverse-proxy access and working dashboard actions

Opened port 8000 to the Nginx Proxy Manager host and repaired three dashboard
buttons that could never have worked.

### Fixed

- **Three HTMX actions targeted routes that do not exist or cannot authenticate.**
  All three pointed at the `/api` JSON router, which is guarded by
  `require_api_access` — a bearer token holding `SECRET_KEY` — that a browser
  carrying only the session cookie cannot present, so the 401 body was swapped
  straight into the page:
  - `articles.html` posted to `/api/ingestion/run`, which no router defines (the
    JSON equivalent is `/api/pipeline/ingest-now`).
  - `sources.html` used `hx-put` for the `skip_media` toggle; the application
    defines no `PUT` route at all.
  - The selector builder posted to `/api/sources/website/test` with `hx-ext="json-enc"`,
    an extension `base.html` never loads, so form-encoded data reached a
    JSON-parsing endpoint.
- All three now post to the cookie-authenticated `/api/ui` partials router and
  render HTML fragments: `POST /api/ui/ingestion/run`,
  `POST /api/ui/sources/{source_id}/skip-media` and
  `POST /api/ui/sources/website/test`. Each reuses the same service function as the
  JSON API, so run-locking, validation and idempotency remain in one place.
- The `skip_media` handler defaults to `False`, because an unchecked HTML checkbox
  submits no field at all — without the default the toggle could only ever switch on.

### Added

- `test_every_htmx_target_in_a_template_is_a_registered_route` parses every
  `hx-*` URL out of the templates and asserts a route accepts that method. Verified
  to reproduce the original failure: restoring the old attribute yields
  `sources.html targets PUT /api/sources/telegram/{{ source.id }}/skip-media, which
  no route accepts`. This class of bug was invisible to 841 passing tests.
- Twelve tests for the new endpoints, covering the held run lock, an unreachable
  Redis, a database failure during the toggle, un-ticking the checkbox, a blank
  selector being dropped rather than sent as an empty string, a partial extraction
  rendering as a success with warnings, and anonymous rejection of all three.
- `hx-disabled-elt` and `hx-indicator` on the two slow actions. Both are
  synchronous — the ingestion pass and a Chromium page load — so a second click
  would otherwise land on the held lock.
- `FakeSession` gained `commit`/`rollback` counters and a scriptable `commit_error`,
  so a handler's rollback path can be asserted rather than assumed.

### Infrastructure

- Port 8000 opened to the NPM host `150.241.98.134` in both `ufw` and the
  `DOCKER-USER` iptables chain, and persisted. The chain rule is the one that
  matters: Docker's nat-table DNAT is evaluated before ufw's INPUT, so a ufw rule
  alone does not filter a published container port.
- **`ufw` had been silently removed from the host.** Installing
  `iptables-persistent` during the 2.0.1 deployment uninstalled it (the two
  conflict), leaving only its orphaned chains — which my own `iptables-save`
  snapshot then persisted. Reinstalled with SSH allowed before enabling, so the
  session could not be locked out. The `DOCKER-USER` rules are what kept the port
  filtered throughout; the DROP counter stood at 318 packets.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` (strict) clean across 57
  source files; 853 tests pass in randomised order (up from 841), 83 of them in
  `tests/test_dashboard_ui.py`
- Live on the VPS after redeploy: the ingestion trigger returns a rendered fragment
  with real counters and a 24 ms duration (previously a 404); the toggle reaches its
  handler and answers 404 for a missing source rather than 401; the selector builder
  started Chromium and previewed a live page in 603 ms with both extraction warnings
- All three endpoints return 401 to an anonymous caller
- Firewall confirmed: 54 packets already accepted from `150.241.98.134`, and port
  8000 still unreachable from an unrelated external address

## [2.1.0] — 2026-08-24 — Multi-account Telegram ingestion & admin profile

Ingestion moves from a single GramJS HTTP bridge to a pool of real Telegram user
accounts over MTProto, and the dashboard login becomes changeable at runtime.

### Added

- **Telegram account pool.** New `telegram_accounts` table (migration `0003`,
  `alembic check` clean) holding `phone_number`, `api_id`, `api_hash`, a
  Fernet-encrypted `session_string`, `is_active`, `last_used_at`,
  `flood_wait_until`, `failure_count` and `last_error`.
  `TelegramAccountPool` hands out the least-recently-used healthy account,
