  persists flood-wait penalties and deactivates an account after three
  consecutive failures.
- **Telethon sign-in with 2FA.** `app/services/telegram_auth.py` drives
  phone → code → cloud password, producing a `StringSession`. The GramJS bridge
  can do neither `StringSession` nor 2FA, which is why Telethon 1.44.0 was added.
- **`scripts/auth_telegram.py`** — interactive CLI with `--list`, bounded retries
  for a mistyped code or password, and upsert by phone number so re-running it
  refreshes an account instead of creating a second rate-limit budget.
- **`app/services/telegram_mtproto.py`** — MTProto reader emitting the *same*
  `TelegramMessage` values as the bridge, so album collapsing, the zero-token
  filter, the dedup ladder and the `ON CONFLICT` upsert are untouched and keep
  their tests. One client per ingestion pass, so a pass uses one pooled account
  rather than claiming a different one per channel.
- **Admin profile at `/profile`.** The username in the navigation bar is now a
  link. Both the username and the password can be changed; each requires the
  current password, and only a bcrypt hash is ever stored.
- **Accounts panel on `/sources`** showing active / flood-wait / auth-required /
  disabled per account. The session string is never rendered.
- `tests/test_telegram_accounts.py` — 54 tests covering encryption, key rotation,
  rotation order, flood-wait accounting, the 2FA branch and the credential store.

### Changed

- **Admin credentials are read from `system_settings`, with `.env` as bootstrap.**
  The spec asked for `.env` to be rewritten; two things made that impossible:
  `/app/.env` does not exist inside the container (Docker passes values via
  `env_file`, verified), and `Settings` is `frozen=True` behind an `lru_cache`, so
  a successful write would not affect the running process until a restart — which
  drops every session. The database row takes effect on the next request.
- `telegram_ingestor._prepare` is now the public `prepare_candidate`, because both
  transports must apply an identical filter; two transports preparing candidates
  differently would write different `raw_news` rows for the same post.

### Fixed

- **Renaming the admin would have locked the operator out.** `decode_session_token`
  compared the token subject against `settings.admin_username` from `.env`, so a
  username changed in the database made every cookie — including the current one —
  invalid, with no way back in. The identity check moved to
  `session_subject_is_current`, which the request guards call with the live name;
  the signature check stays synchronous. A rename still ends sessions issued under
  the old name, which is the property worth keeping.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` (strict) clean across
  63 source files; 907 tests pass in randomised order (up from 853)
- `alembic check` on the deployed database reports no drift. A first pass declared
  `unique=True, index=True` on the column while the migration created a separate
  constraint plus a non-unique index; the model now declares the named constraint
  and the redundant index was dropped
- Password change exercised live: a wrong current password and a mismatched
  confirmation are refused with 400, a valid change takes effect on the next login
  with no restart, the old password stops working, and only `$2b$12$…` is stored
- `+79055342403` authorised through the full code + 2FA path (user 8228066186,
  @Genadich2403). The pool then claimed the account, decrypted its session,
  connected and read 50 live messages from `@telegram`; `plaintext_in_db=False`
  and the ciphertext appears zero times in the rendered page

### Notes

- Account authorisation is a CLI step, not a dashboard button. Telegram delivers a
  code and may then demand the cloud password, so it needs a live interactive
  prompt; a fire-and-forget HTTP request has nowhere to ask. The panel shows the
  command instead of a button that could not finish the job.
- Rotation across two accounts and a real `FloodWaitError` are covered by unit
  tests but not yet observed live — both need a second authorised account.

## [2.2.0] — 2026-08-24 — OmniRoute gateway, selectable models, first live pipeline run

The pipeline generated its first real articles: 151 posts collected from three
Telegram channels through the pooled account, 143 clusters, 27 drafts written by
`auto/fast` through the OmniRoute gateway.

### Fixed

- **Response scaffolding leaked into the published article body.** `auto/fast`
  occasionally closes the `content_html` string with the wrong quote, so the
  remaining response keys become part of the body. The reply is still *valid JSON*
  and every other field is present, which is why nothing upstream caught it:
  `json.loads` succeeds, the schema validates, the slop filter sees ordinary text.
  Four of the first eleven articles ended with an English image prompt appended to
  their Russian text, and would have been published that way.
  `_body_violations` now rejects a body carrying a leaked field, which triggers the
  existing retry. Rejected rather than trimmed: the boundary between article and
  scaffolding cannot be recovered reliably, and a retry costs one call while a
  silently truncated article is published wrong. The four contaminated articles
  were deleted and regenerated clean.
- **The worker panel misreported ingestion.** It checked `GRAMJS_TOKEN`, so a
  deployment collecting happily through the MTProto account pool was shown as
  unconfigured — the panel was describing a fallback nobody uses. It now reports
  the pool, distinguishing "no accounts" from "accounts exist but none is usable",
  because the operator action differs.

### Added

- **Selectable models per pipeline stage.** `app/services/model_routing.py` stores
  overrides in `system_settings`, with the environment as the fallback, following
  the same pattern as the admin credentials and for the same reasons. The settings
  page offers an editable matrix with autocompletion from the gateway's own
  catalogue, so a mistyped model is visible before it becomes a failed generation.
  An empty field clears an override and restores the default.
- `AIClient.list_models` plus a non-retrying `_get`: making an operator wait through
  a backoff ladder for an optional autocompletion list is the wrong trade.
- The writer model is resolved per cluster, so a change mid-run affects the next
  cluster instead of requiring the run to finish.
- 21 tests: model routing (fallback, per-role independence, validation, malformed
  document) and the leak guard, including that Russian quoted speech — which the
  news copy is full of — does not trigger a false positive.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 64 source files; 922 tests pass
- OmniRoute verified from inside the container: 1315-model catalogue, live completion
- Live run: 151 raw posts → 143 clusters → 27 articles, **0 leaked bodies** after the
  fix, confirmed by querying both quote styles across every article
- The zero-token filter rejected 8 posts (7 digests, 1 advertisement) before any API
  call, which is the economics the design depends on
- Model change exercised end to end: an identifier with a space is refused with 400,
  a valid one is stored in `system_settings` and applies without a restart

### Notes

- **Embeddings are unavailable on this gateway.** `/embeddings` answers `403` for
  Gemini ("project has been denied access") and `400` for OpenAI ("no credentials
  for embedding provider"). Dedup level 1 (MinHash, 0 tokens) is unaffected; level 2
  degrades exactly as designed — an ambiguous post becomes a new cluster and the run
  continues. `articles.final_embedding` stays null for all 27 articles.
- `FAL_KEY` is unset, so AI covers are skipped with a warning per cluster.
- One dedup latency test is timing-sensitive and fails under machine load while
  passing in isolation; it exercises MinHash, not anything changed here.

## [2.2.1] — 2026-08-24 — Ingestion worker contract fixed and pinned

Four ingestion rules audited against the Telethon reader. Two were already correct,
two were missing entirely and are now implemented, and all four are covered by tests
that were each verified to fail when the implementation is broken.

### Added

- **Entity filtering (rule 1, was missing).** The reader accepted whatever
  `get_entity` returned, so a source pointing at a group would have had its
  conversational traffic republished as news — both wrong and a privacy problem.
  `is_broadcast_channel` now admits public and private broadcast channels only;
  megagroups, gigagroups, small groups, private chats and bots are refused with an
  error naming what was found, because the source needs fixing rather than retrying.
  A `gigagroup` is the subtle case: Telegram converts it from a megagroup and it
  reports `broadcast=True`, so checking that flag alone would let it through.
- **Read acknowledgement (rule 4, was lost).** `MTProtoIngestor.mark_read` calls
  `send_read_acknowledge(entity, max_id=...)`, and the worker calls it **after** the
  batch commits. The ordering is the point: acknowledging first and then failing the
  commit would leave posts read in Telegram but absent from the database, invisible
  to an operator scanning the channel for what the pipeline missed. `_Counters` now
  carries `max_message_id` out of the collector so the call can happen post-commit.

### Changed

- **First pass no longer applies the overlap offset (rule 2).** With no watermark
  there is nothing to overlap with, and subtracting from zero only shrank an already
  bounded window. A first pass now takes exactly `INGEST_MAX_MESSAGES_PER_SOURCE`
  (50) most recent posts with `min_id=0`.
- Later passes keep `min_id` from the watermark minus the overlap window (rule 3).
  `min_id` is exclusive in Telethon, so this returns new posts only; the overlap is
  deliberate, because Telegram allows a post to be edited and its media attached
  late, and repeats are absorbed by the `(source_id, external_post_id)` constraint.
- The GramJS bridge no longer marks read inside `ingest_source`. It happened before
  the commit and would have double-fired alongside the worker's call; `mark_read`
  stays public for the worker.
- `_resolve_channel` split out of `fetch_new_messages`, keeping resolution failures
  and reading failures separately readable.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 64 source files; 938 tests pass
- Each rule proven by deliberate breakage: removing the entity check, restoring the
  first-pass offset and dropping the ascending sort each fail their own test
- Live against Telegram: `@tass_agency` and `@telegram` accepted as channels;
  `@python`, `@durovschat` (супергруппы) and `@BotFather` (бот) refused; first pass
  returned 50 posts (391726..391775) in ascending order; a pass with
  `last_max_id=391775` returned only the 10-message overlap window;
  `send_read_acknowledge` returned True and logged `Marked tass_agency read up to
  message 391775`
- The no-op paths were exercised too: a website source and an empty batch make no
  Telegram call at all

## [2.3.0] — 2026-08-24 — Specification alignment: categories, styles, local embeddings

The first three sections of the pipeline specification are now fixed in code. Two
of them contradicted what was measured, and the deviations are recorded below.

### Added

- **`app/services/categories.py`** — the 20-category catalogue with WordPress term
  ids, six rewrite voices, moderation overrides and body-format rules. Previously
  the code carried 20 categories of which **18 slugs did not match the
  specification**, and no WordPress term ids at all, so publishing could not target
  a section deliberately.
- **Local embeddings** via fastembed (`paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2`, 384
  dims) in `app/services/local_embeddings.py`. Vectors now cost nothing and need no
  network. This is not only an optimisation: OmniRoute has no embedding provider at
  all — `403` for Gemini, `400` for OpenAI — so level 2 deduplication was inert.
- `RiskLevel.SPAM.blocks_generation`, so an advertisement is dropped before any
  model is called rather than after.
- `tests/test_categories.py` — 54 tests. The specification's map is duplicated in
  the test file on purpose: a test importing the value it checks would pass no
  matter what that value became. Verified by mutation — changing one term id from
  2 to 22 fails the test.

### Changed

- `RiskLevel` from low/medium/high/critical to **green/yellow/red/spam**, matching
  the moderator's own output.
- `ArticleStatus` gains **`media_planned`** between `draft_tag` and `media_ready`.
  Without it a retry cannot distinguish "media never planned" from "planned but the
  build failed".
- Deduplication window 72h -> **48h**, per the specification.
- Vector columns **1536 -> 384** (migration `0004_spec_alignment`), with the HNSW
  indexes dropped and rebuilt. Existing vectors are discarded rather than cast: a
  vector from another model is not a projection of the same space, so keeping them
  would leave cosine distance meaningless while looking valid. They are derived
  data and every row is re-embeddable from its stored text.
- `sources` gains `fetch_media`, `telegram_id`, `avatar_data_uri` and
  `posts_collected`, the last backfilled from `raw_news`.

### Deviations from the specification, with evidence

- **Similarity thresholds are 0.55/0.76, not 0.75/0.92.** Measured over 6 paraphrase
  pairs and 5 unrelated pairs of real Russian news, the pinned model scores
  duplicates at 0.765-0.911 and unrelated stories at -0.07-0.713. The specification's
  0.92 sits *above every measured duplicate*, so every one of them would have gone
  to the paid LLM arbiter — the opposite of a zero-token stage. At 0.76 the free
  path catches 6/6 duplicates with 0 false merges; 0.78 and above starts losing
  duplicates for no accuracy gain.
- **A sixth rewrite voice was added.** The specification's five cover 18 of the 20
  categories; astrology/esoterica and esports were left out and would have fallen
  back to the incident register, which is the wrong tone for a horoscope.

### Fixed during deployment

- **The deploy crash-looped every container.** `AI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS=1536` in the
  server `.env` no longer matched the 384-wide schema, and the settings validator
  correctly refuses to boot on that — so the containers were replaced and all six
  failed at once. `deploy_vps.sh` now checks the width before touching the running
  stack; verified to catch exactly this.
- **The model cache volume was root-owned.** `/app/storage/fastembed` was absent
  from the Dockerfile's `mkdir`/`chown`, so Docker created it as root and the
  non-root runtime user could not write to it. The failure surfaced as
  `Could not load model ... from any source`, which reads like a network problem;
  the real cause was `Permission denied`.
- Worker containers kept the stale `AI_EMBEDDING_DIMENSIONS` after `restart`:
  `env_file` is read at container *creation*, so they needed `--force-recreate`.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 66 source files; 992 tests pass
- Migration applied on the VPS after a `pg_dump` backup: vectors confirmed
  `vector(384)`, `risk_level` enum swapped, `media_planned` present, four columns
  added, both HNSW indexes rebuilt, `posts_collected` backfilled (96/61/64)
- Local embeddings confirmed inside the worker container: first call 8.1 s including
  the model download, then 0.04 s for three texts; 241 MB cached in the volume
- Live thresholds re-checked in-container: a paraphrase pair scores 0.765
  (duplicate, free) and an unrelated pair -0.07 (new cluster, free)

### Still outstanding

Sections 1, 2 and 3 of the specification — the model fallback pool, the sources
channel manager and the articles page rework — plus wiring the moderation prompt to
return the specified JSON. Tracked as modules 10.5-10.8 in `TODO.md`.

## [2.4.0] — 2026-08-25 — Sources channel manager and articles page rework

Sections 2 and 3 of the specification are implemented: the sources page is now a
channel manager driven by the live Telethon session, and the articles page gained
pagination, filters, thumbnails, status grids and a preview modal.

### Added

- **Sources page.** `MTProtoIngestor.list_channels()` walks the account's dialog
  list and returns exactly the peers ingestion may read — broadcast channels only,
  groups and private chats filtered by the same predicate the reader enforces.
  Avatars arrive as small data URIs so a hundred-row table needs no Telegram
  round-trip. Import (`POST /api/ui/sources/telegram/import`) upserts by identifier:
  existing sources keep their operator-set flags, new ones arrive **inactive**,
  because importing a hundred dialogs must not silently start collecting from all
  of them. Per-row checkboxes post both flags in one request and swap in the row
  re-rendered from the database; bulk buttons cover select-all, clear-all and
  media-only.
- **Articles page.** Pagination at 20 rows (count query shares the filter
  predicates, so the pager reflects what the filters leave); filters for rubric,
  status, period and has-media plus title search; each row carries a thumbnail
  (302 to the WordPress thumbnail, or an inline SVG placeholder — proxying bytes
  through the dashboard would double bandwidth for no benefit), a colour-grouped
  category badge, the DD.MM.YYYY HH:MM timestamp, a readiness grid (rewrite /
  tags / media) and a distribution grid (WP / TG / VK / Dzen chips read
  defensively from `platform_statuses`). Clicking a row opens a modal: rendered
  HTML in a sandboxed pane beside a quick-edit form whose category select keeps
  `wp_category_id` in step with the slug.
- Category helpers registered as Jinja globals, so every list showing a rubric can
  render its Russian title without threading them through each route's context.

### Fixed

- **The initial channels table rendered empty** although import had stored 33
  channels: the route passed `channel_rows` while the partial iterates `rows`. One
  name now serves both paths.
- **Bulk action returned 500**: `Source.source_type.is_(...)` compiles to
  `IS`, which asyncpg rejects for an enum parameter; equality is correct.
- **The preview modal crashed with `'str' object is not callable`**: its context
  passed `category_title` as a string value, shadowing the global helper of the
  same name inside the template.
- The route-guard test caught my own `hx-get="#"` close button as a target no
  route accepts — replaced with plain JS.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 66 source files; 993 tests pass
- Live on the VPS: import discovered 33 broadcast channels and stored them; the
  table renders 108 rows; clear-all left 0 active in the database; a single toggle
  re-renders with both flags checked; the modal opens (200) and a POST to edit
  persists; the thumbnail endpoint answers; filtering by rubric narrows the table;
  pagination shows «стр. 1 из 15 · всего 290»

### Still outstanding

Section 1 — the model fallback pool with a 60-second cooldown and its settings UI.

## [2.5.0] — 2026-08-25 — Лаборатория: observable runs, and a production switch

The pipeline stopped being a black box. An operator can now run one news item from
collection to finished article and see, per stage, what went in, which model was
asked, what came back and *why* each decision was made.

### Added

- **`app/services/pipeline_stages.py`** — the stage contract (`StageResult`,
  `RunSummary`) and `ArtifactStore`. Artifacts are files under
  `storage/runs/<run_id>/NN-stage.json`, not database columns: the evidence worth
  showing differs per stage and grows with every question an operator asks, and a
  JSONB column would make each UI addition a migration. The layout follows the
  Excalibur pipeline's per-article artifact folders. History is bounded at 50 runs
  because a run holds prompts plus a full article on the volume the media pipeline
  shares.
- **`app/services/pipeline_runner.py`** — the orchestrator: collect → dedup →
  cluster → research → article → media. Every stage calls the *same* service the
  production worker calls; a test-only copy of the dedup ladder would eventually
  disagree with the real one and the operator would be inspecting a lie.
- **Research stage**, taken from the Excalibur design and adapted for a news desk:
  archive facts plus previously published articles on the same topic, matched by
  headline-word overlap. Token-free and explainable in the UI, which matters more
  here than recall. It also answers "have we covered this before, and is the link
  in the text" — reporting interlink candidates and, honestly, that embedding them
  into the body is not implemented yet.
- **`/lab` page** with two buttons: «Сбор + прогон» and «Прогон без сбора».
  Re-running generation without pulling fresh posts is the common case while
  tuning prompts. The timeline shows per stage: verdict, model, latency, warnings,
  the system prompt verbatim, and the rendered article. Dedup rows show similarity,
  ladder level and *the threshold crossed* — a bare similarity number explains
  nothing without its comparison.
- **`app/services/pipeline_control.py`** — production switch and collection
  interval in `system_settings`, cached in Redis for 10s. The switch is checked in
  the cron tasks, not inside the pipeline functions, so a paused pipeline still
  allows lab runs. It **fails open**: an unreachable database must not silently
  pause collection, because a paused pipeline keeps reporting healthy and the lost
  news cycle is only noticed hours later.
- `/settings` section for the switch and the interval (5–120 minutes, default 15
  per the specification). Cron minutes are *derived* from the interval rather than
  stored, so the two cannot disagree; an interval that does not divide 60 still
  produces a valid schedule with a shorter final gap.
- 36 tests covering artifacts, path-traversal rejection, pruning, dedup verdict
  wording, and the switch's fail-open and clamping behaviour.

### Fixed

- **A placeholder rubric reached generation.** Deduplication opened new clusters
  with `category="general"`, which is not one of the 20 rubrics; with the moderator
  stage not yet wired in, that value travelled all the way into the writer and
  would have reached `wp_category_id` as the fallback term. Observed on a live run.
  New clusters now default to `novosti-obshhestva`.
- **The run page returned 500 after a successful 23-second pass.** The media stage
  rendered `d.items`, which Jinja resolves to the bound `dict.items` method rather
  than to the key of that name. Subscripted now, with a test pinning it — the
  attribute form fails only at render time and only on runs that have media.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 69 source files; 1029 tests
  pass in randomised order
- Live on the VPS: `/lab` renders, a run without collection completed in 23 s and
  produced all five stages with the prompt and thresholds visible and the token
  spend flagged; artifacts written as `01-dedup.json` … `05-media.json` plus
  `run.json`; the switch round-tripped through `system_settings`
  (`{"enabled": false, "interval_minutes": 15}`) and re-enabling reported cron
  minutes `0, 15, 30, 45`

### Notes

- Publishing modules (Telegram / Дзен / VK) are not yet part of the lab timeline;
  the stage names exist in the contract so adding an adapter is a registration, not
  a redesign.
- Watermark verdicts are reported as "analysis only" in test mode: nothing is
  downloaded, so the detector runs on the production pass when a file exists.

## [2.5.1] — 2026-08-25 — Publishing modules in the lab timeline

The lab now shows all eight stages. The three distribution modules reshape the
article for their platform and display the result; a test run still posts nothing.

### Added

- **M6 Telegram**: caption built by the production `build_caption` — HTML subset,
  hashtags, link button — with its length shown against Telegram's 1024 UTF-16
  unit limit for photo captions.
- **M7 Дзен**: body reduced by `build_dzen_html` to the tag subset Dzen accepts,
  with before/after lengths, so an operator sees what the filter removed.
- **M8 ВКонтакте**: wall text by `build_wall_message`. The URL is deliberately
  absent from the text — it travels as an attachment so VK renders a preview card —
  and a test asserts that, because a link pasted into the body would suppress the
  card.
- Each module reuses the production builder rather than approximating it, so the
  timeline shows what the publisher would actually send. Every panel states that a
  test run does not post.

### Fixed

- **An unreachable warning.** The Telegram stage warned when the caption hit the
  1024-unit limit, but `build_caption` reduces the body to a teaser long before
  that — measured at 94 units for a 6000-character article, so the branch could
  never fire. Replaced with the signal that is actually useful: a caption under a
  quarter of the limit usually means the article had little plain prose, such as a
  gallery post.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 69 source files; 1032 tests
- Live on the VPS: one run produced all eight stages in 45 s — Дедупликация,
  Кластер, Ресерч, Статья, Обработка медиа, Модуль Telegram, Модуль Дзен, Модуль
  ВКонтакте — with the three "nothing is posted" guarantees visible in the panels

## [2.6.0] — 2026-08-25 — Moderation before generation

The pipeline gained the stage its original n8n design had and the Python port had
lost: a moderator that classifies a cluster and drops advertising *before* any
paid call.

### Added

- **`app/services/moderator.py`** — one call returns the rubric, the risk level
  (`green`/`yellow`/`red`/`spam`) and a one-sentence reason. The rubric list in the
  prompt and the JSON schema enum are generated from `CATEGORY_MAP`, so a rubric
  added to the catalogue cannot be missing from the prompt — the model would
  otherwise invent a slug with no WordPress term.
- **Spam is dropped before generation.** An advertising post used to cost a writer
  call, an SEO pass, a cover and a WordPress upload before anyone noticed. The
  cluster is marked processed so it does not return on every tick.
- **The two absolute rules are enforced in code, not only in the prompt.** Crime,
  accidents and loss of life are always incidents; drones, air defence and the
  front are always military. A prompt is guidance and the pipeline needs a
  guarantee — a model that sees a car files a fatal crash under motoring. The
  override is shown in the timeline with the rubric the model proposed, rather than
  silently corrected.
- **`risk_level` now reaches the writer.** `yellow` appends the softening
  instruction (remove profanity, graphic detail and gratuitous violence, keep the
  facts); incidents and military stories get the emergency shape — short, safety
  first, no analysis. Both are appended to the *user* turn, because they describe
  this story rather than the editorial voice.
- Moderation stage in the lab timeline: rubric, risk chip, whether generation is
  allowed, the reason, the override note and the full prompt.
- 23 moderator tests plus a stub in the content-worker fixture.

### Fixed

- **The gateway streams unconditionally, and the client read only the first
  chunk.** OmniRoute answers `text/event-stream` even without `stream: true`; read
  as plain JSON that yields two characters — `'{"'` — which surfaced as "the
  moderator returned invalid JSON" on three of five probe cases while the model had
  answered correctly. `AIClient._decode` now reassembles an SSE body into the
  non-streaming shape, so streaming stays invisible above that method.
- **A reasoning model spent the whole token budget thinking.** `auto/fast` routes
  to models that reason in the visible content field: 393 of 400 completion tokens
  went to a preamble and the JSON never arrived, with `finish_reason: stop`. The
  moderator budget is 1200 tokens and the prompt forbids any text around the JSON.
- **The lab picked media-only clusters.** A cluster holding just a video has no
  text, so moderation and generation both skipped and the run showed nothing
  useful — which is exactly what the first live moderation run produced. Cluster
  selection now requires a post with text.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 70 source files; 1055 tests
- Moderator probed against the live gateway on five hand-built cases: a Moscow
  metro story went to `novosti-moskvy`, a fatal MKAD crash to `chp-i-proisshestvij`
  (overriding the model's motoring answer), a drone report to `voennye-novosti`, a
  promo-code post to `spam` with generation blocked, and a rate decision to
  `ekonomika`
- Live lab run: nine stages, moderation returned «Новости политики» / `green` with
  its prompt and artifact recorded

## [2.7.0] — 2026-08-27 — The fallback pool

Every AI stage was a single model, so one provider hiccup stopped the pipeline.
Each stage now carries an ordered chain, and a model that just failed is routed
around instead of being retried into the ground.

### Added

- **`app/services/model_pool.py`** — `run_with_fallback` walks the chain
  primary → fallback 1 → fallback 2 and returns the value together with the route
  taken, so the timeline can show which model actually served the call.
- **An unusable reply counts as a failure.** Invalid JSON, a truncated document, a
  schema violation: from the caller's side these are indistinguishable from a 500,
  because in both cases this model cannot do the job right now. The `validate` hook
  lets a caller reject a syntactically fine answer and have it treated as that
  model failing. This is not hypothetical — it is precisely what this gateway does
  when a reasoning model spends its whole token budget thinking.
- **A configuration error is not retried.** 400, 401, 403 and 404 repeat
  identically on every model in the chain; cycling it would waste two more calls
  and bury the real cause behind two lookalike failures. Such an error is raised
  immediately.
- **The cooldown lives in Redis, not in the process.** Three worker containers and
  the web tier all call the same gateway; a per-process cooldown would let each of
  them discover the same dead model independently, at three times the failed calls
  and three times the latency. Redis failures are swallowed: losing a cooldown
  costs one wasted call, while raising would abort a run because the cache blinked.
- **The cooldown is keyed per role.** A model that is healthy for a short
  classification can fail on a long article, so `arbiter` and `writer` penalties are
  independent — the same name failing under one role says nothing about the other.
- **Sixty seconds, flat.** The failures this guards against are transient: a rate
  window, a gateway 502, a truncated stream. A growing penalty would slowly migrate
  every stage onto its worst model.
- **A fully cooling chain still attempts the primary.** The cooldown is an
  optimisation, not a guarantee, and a stalled pipeline is worse than one wasted
  call.
- **A skipped model is reported as skipped, not failed.** `Attempt.skipped`
  distinguishes "in cooldown, never asked" from "called and refused", so the
  timeline does not blame a model for a call it never received.
- **`probe_models` and the «Проверить модели» button.** The gateway advertises
  hundreds of models and says nothing about which serve *this* account, so the only
  honest way to build a chain is to call each one. An empty reply is reported as a
  failure rather than a success, because that is this gateway's characteristic mode
  and a model answering nothing must not end up in a chain. Concurrency is bounded
  at 4: twenty simultaneous requests would measure the gateway's queue rather than
  the models. A probe never raises — one dead name must not abort the sweep.
- **`load_chains` / `save_chains` in `model_routing.py`**, `CHAIN_LENGTH = 3`. A
  role with no stored chain falls back to a single-element chain holding its
  resolved model, so the single-model path keeps working without a migration. Each
  chain is de-duplicated while preserving order: the same model twice means a
  failure is retried against itself, which is what the cooldown exists to avoid.
- **`/settings` section** — the per-stage chain table, «Проверить модели» and
  «Сбросить кулдауны», and a report with per-model latency and the live cooldowns.
  The reset exists for the operator who has just fixed the provider and does not
  want to wait out a penalty the system no longer needs.
- **Wired into production, not only the lab.** `moderate_with_fallback` runs the
  moderator through the `arbiter` chain; the writer runs through the `writer` chain
  in `content_worker`. Reaching a fallback writes a warning naming the model into
  the article report, so a degraded run is visible rather than merely slower. An
  exhausted pool fails the article, not the run: the next tick may find the models
  recovered.
- 21 pool tests, including one asserting the flat minute, one proving a
  configuration error stops after the first model, and one proving a fully cooling
  chain still tries the primary.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 70 source files; 1076 tests
  pass
- Local stack brought up from `docker-compose.prod.yml`: postgres, redis, app and
  the three ARQ workers

### Outstanding

- The pool has not yet been exercised against the live gateway. SSH to the VPS
  refuses the key exchange (`kex_exchange_identification`) while the host still
  answers ping and accepts a connection on 22, so v2.7.0 is not deployed and
  «Проверить модели» has not been run against real models. Before restarting
  `sshd`, check `df -h` and `journalctl -u sshd`: a full disk or an OOM kill
  produces this same symptom, and a reboot would mask it rather than fix it.
- The repository has no commits. The whole tree is untracked, so this version
  exists only in the working directory.

## [2.8.0] — 2026-08-31 — Queue reset and the zero-token intake inspection

The Лаборатория could walk one cluster to a finished article, which answers a
different question from the one an operator actually has first: *is the intake
right?* Seeing that means seeing every post that arrived from every channel, which
of them were rejected and by which rule, which photos carry a channel watermark,
and what the archive already knows about the stories that survived. None of that
needs a paid model, and now none of it uses one.

### Added

- **`app/services/pipeline_reset.py`** — clears the collected queue: `raw_news`,
  clusters with no article, the Redis LSH buckets, the cached signatures and the
  model cooldowns.
- **The reset rewinds the per-source watermark**, and that is the point rather than
  a detail. `sources.last_scraped_at` and `posts_collected` go back to zero, so
  `_telegram_watermark` returns `None` and the reader takes its first-pass branch:
  exactly `INGEST_MAX_MESSAGES_PER_SOURCE` posts from **every** channel. "50 posts
  from all channels" is therefore a consequence of the reset, not a second
  collection mode nobody would maintain.
- **Clusters carrying an article survive, and the report says what that costs.**
  `articles.cluster_id` is `NOT NULL` with `ON DELETE CASCADE`, so deleting such a
  cluster deletes a published article whose live WordPress post no database row can
  unpublish. Keeping it has a visible consequence: the cluster stays in the level 2
  index, so a re-collected post about that story lands in duplicates instead of
  opening a new cluster. `include_articles=True` is the separate, explicit choice.
- **A reset is refused while the ingestion lock is held.** Mid-pass it would delete
  the rows that pass is deduplicating against, leaving an LSH index that disagrees
  with the table — the one state the dedup ladder cannot explain.
- **PostgreSQL first, Redis second.** A leftover bucket whose row is gone makes
  deduplication *over*-report duplicates, which the next run shows; clearing the
  index while the rows survive would make it *under*-report them silently.
- **«Очистить очередь» on `/lab`, in both modes.** In test mode it is how the
  current thresholds get re-checked against the same channels; in production it is
  how a misconfigured run is undone without waiting out the 48-hour window. The
  production switch is deliberately not consulted — a paused pipeline is exactly
  when an operator needs to clear what the last run collected.
- **`app/services/pipeline_inspection.py`** — collect → dedup → watermarks →
  research, with no paid call anywhere in the path. Safe to press repeatedly while
  tuning, which is what makes threshold work possible at all.
- **The dedup report is evidence, not a counter.** Every post carries its channel,
  the verdict in words, the ladder level, the similarity, *the threshold it was
  compared against*, the zero-token filter rules that fired, and every attached
  media item. A bare similarity number explains nothing.
- **A watermark stage that actually looks.** This is the one thing metadata cannot
  answer, so the photos are downloaded and analysed: clean / croppable / rejected /
  unchecked, with the OCR text and the coverage ratio behind each verdict. Tesseract
  and the overlay mask run locally and cost nothing; the *download* is the expensive
  part, so it is bounded twice — `MAX_WATERMARK_IMAGES` per run and
  `MAX_IMAGE_BYTES` per file, with duplicate `media_id` values collapsed so one
  photograph reposted by three channels does not consume the budget.
- **`MTProtoIngestor.download_photo`** — per-message fetch with a size ceiling, one
  pooled client for the whole stage rather than one per image.
- **"Never looked" is kept apart from "looked and found nothing".** Both produce no
  watermark and only one means the image is publishable, so a source with media
  collection off or `TEXT_ONLY` reports `unchecked`, while `ALWAYS_REGENERATE`
  reports `rejected` because production genuinely will not use the original.
- **The research stage covers every cluster**, not just the one being written:
  facts, the source texts they came from, the cluster's media, and the archive
  matches over 30 days with the overlap count that justifies each one.
- **`app/services/research.py`** — `collect_facts` and `find_related_articles`,
  extracted so the lab run and the inspection call the same code. Two copies would
  eventually disagree, leaving an operator comparing two "research" panels that mean
  different things.
- **Lab buttons ordered by cost.** The two free passes come first; the two that
  spend tokens are visually separated and confirm before running.
- 34 inspection tests, including one asserting no inspection stage reports
  `tokens_spent`, one proving the reset rewinds the watermark, and one proving a
  refused reset leaves the LSH index intact.

### Fixed

- **The test suite inherited the developer's `.env`.** `Settings` reads
  `.env` at first instantiation and is cached and frozen from then on, so adding
  `ALLOWED_HOSTS` locally registered `TrustedHostMiddleware` and answered every
  dashboard request to `http://dashboard.test` with "Invalid host header" — 60
  failures that said nothing about the code under test. `conftest.py` now pins the
  variable before any `app` import, which makes the suite deterministic on any
  machine rather than only on one without that setting.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 73 source files; 1111 tests
  pass in random order
- Local stack previously brought up from `docker-compose.prod.yml` (postgres, redis,
  app and the three ARQ workers); the inspection path has not yet been exercised
  against live channels, because that still needs the VPS

### Outstanding

- The pool and the inspection remain unexercised against the live gateway and live
  channels. SSH to the VPS still refuses the key exchange
  (`kex_exchange_identification`) while answering ping and accepting a connection on
  22. Before restarting `sshd`, check `df -h` and `journalctl -u sshd`: a full disk
  or an OOM kill produces the same symptom, and a reboot would mask it.

## [2.9.0] — 2026-08-31 — Writing the cluster the operator chose

The inspection showed the whole intake but ended there, so the only way to write was
still "whichever cluster is newest" — the one choice reading the intake was supposed
to inform. The timeline now hands over.

### Added

- **`PipelineRunner.run(cluster_id=...)`** writes a named cluster instead of the
  newest unwritten one, and **«Написать статью»** appears on every cluster in the
  research report. `collect=0`, because the posts are already stored and
  re-collecting would add Telegram traffic for nothing.
- **A cluster that already carries an article offers no button.** Paying a writer
  call, an SEO pass and a cover for a story that is already written is the mistake
  worth designing against, not a warning worth showing afterwards.
- **A missing cluster fails the stage rather than falling back to "newest".** The id
  comes from a page that may be minutes old: the queue can be reset or another pass
  can write that cluster in between. Silently writing a *different* story than the
  one chosen is worse than writing nothing, so the stage says the cluster is gone and
  asks for a fresh inspection. A malformed id is refused with 422 for the same
  reason.
- **`chosen_by_operator` on the cluster stage**, so a run replayed from artifacts
  states whether the story was picked deliberately or taken as the newest.
- 4 tests: the per-cluster handover, the suppressed button for a written cluster, the
  422 on a malformed id, and the two new lab endpoints added to the anonymous-caller
  guard.

### Verification

- `ruff check .` clean; `mypy` (strict) clean across 73 source files; 1115 tests pass
  in random order

## [2.9.1] — 2026-09-03 — Documentation as a maintained system

The project rules required reading `docs/`, but seven files named by the rules did
not exist, two existing documents were ten-line stubs, and several described a
past implementation rather than the code now running. That made a new session
choose between guessing and claiming it had read a document that was not there.

### Changed

- `AGENTS.md` and `CLAUDE.md` now make documentation a completion criterion: read
  the module document before changing code; update it in the same task; create it
  for substantial modules; never describe planned behaviour as implemented.
- Created `docs/05_CONTENT_ENGINE.md` through `docs/11_DEPLOYMENT_DEVOPS.md`.
  Each separates the verified current implementation from the approved future
  work in `PROJECT.md`.
- Replaced the `00_INDEX.md`, `01_DATA_PIPELINE.md`, and `02_DATABASE_SPEC.md`
  stubs with an accurate system map, state-machine description, and schema guide.
- Corrected existing ingestion/dedup documentation: Telethon is the production
  path (GramJS is fallback), first-pass limit is 50, vectors are local 384-wide,
  semantic thresholds are 0.55/0.76 over 48 hours, and the direct external
  embedding call that disables level-2 deduplication is documented as a defect.
- Documented the approved product brief, phase plan, OAuth requirement, WordPress
  SSH requirement, manual-first operating mode, Wordstat/SEO flow, and per-article
  run journal in `PROJECT.md`, `STATE.md`, and the current TODO section.

### Verification

- Every `docs/` reference in project Markdown resolves to an existing file.
- Every newly created module document has distinct `Реализовано` and
  `Запланировано` sections.
- `ruff check .` clean.
- `mypy app scripts migrations/env.py` clean across 74 source files.
